API & Expansion Manual

The interfaces of the SolariNet monitoring and system-of-record platform: REST, control plane, change-data capture, SoR, status panel and provisioning. Also covers how to extend the platform, how it is maintained, and where it is heading.

As of main a28bdf2 · 2026-11-03 · live release rc-20261103-c39f565-io
Supersedes SolariNet_Inventory_API.html in substance. Operator: Jason C. Kay. Internal — no secrets are reproduced; credentials are referenced by path only.

SolariNet · Maintainer & integrator reference

API & Expansion Manual

As of main a28bdf2 · 2026-11-03 · live release rc-20261103-c39f565-io · 194 HTTP routes · supersedes SolariNet_Inventory_API.html

0About this manual

This manual is the reference for every interface SolariNet exposes. It is also the procedure book for extending the platform without breaking it. It is written for maintainers, integrators and anyone adding a screen, route, migration, applier, probe or channel.

0.1Scope and baseline

Source baseline
main at a28bdf2 (2026-11-03). Every route, verb and table named here was read from that tree.
Live baseline
Release rc-20261103-c39f565-io under /opt/solarinet/releases/ on xenon. The SoR ledger head on cesium is 037_inventory_items_closeout (applied 2026-11-03 21:18 UTC), checked on 2026-11-03. Passages marked Since 037 were verified against the merged 037 source on main.
Covers
The HTTP API (194 routes), the C control socket and SCP wire protocol, CDC over RabbitMQ, the SoR as a read surface, the status-panel contract, provisioning and Ansible hooks, notification, and configuration names. It also covers extension recipes, maintenance discipline and the roadmap.
Supersedes
docs/SolariNet_Inventory_API.html (rev. 1.4). Its pagination, compatibility, product-catalogue and location content is folded into §3 Inventory and §2 Pagination. That file is left unedited.
Companions
The User Manual covers operating the dashboard. The Developer Manual covers the build and internals. This manual covers the contracts between components and how to add to them.

0.2Conventions

  • Verified vs inferred. Statements are verified against code or the live system unless they are tagged INFERRED or placed in a grey Inference box.
  • Since 037. A note headed Since 037 describes inventory behaviour introduced by migration 037 (live 2026-11-03).
  • Roles. public means no session. session means any signed-in principal, including viewer. operator means role operator or admin. admin is admin only.
  • Secrets. No credential, token, key or option-file content appears here. Where a secret is needed, the manual names the file or environment variable that holds it.
  • Source references are path:line against the baseline commit. Line numbers drift; the symbol names do not.
  • Hostnames follow the periodic-table convention: xenon, cesium, benzene, lithium, radium, chlorine, steel.

0.3How to use it

If you are…Start at
Calling the API from a script or tool§2 REST fundamentals, then §11.9 Integrating an external tool
Looking up one endpointType into the filter box (press /), or use Appendix B
Adding a feature§11 Extension recipes and the extension-point map
Changing the SoR schema§12.3 Migration discipline
Consuming change events§5 CDC and messaging
Planning work§13 Roadmap

1System surfaces and hosts

SolariNet is a C monitoring tier (server, clients, monitors), a PHP dashboard and API, a MariaDB System of Record (SoR), and a set of Python services joined by RabbitMQ. Each has its own interface. This chapter places them on hosts so the later chapters can refer to them by name.

1.1Hosts and what they serve

HostRole in SolariNetInterfaces it exposes
xenonPrimary server. Runs Apache and PHP 8.5.4 (dashboard + API), solariServer and a local client and monitor, and the monitoring DB (MariaDB 11.8.6). Also runs sor-emit, sor-apply-dns, sor-reconcile, unifipolld, notify, alertbridge and the BIND primary for akoria.net.HTTPS dashboard.akoria.net; SCP TLS on 7701/7702/7703; ctl socket /run/solari/solariCtl.sock
cesiumSoR host (MariaDB 12.3.2, database solari). Also runs the Forgejo git server and the nightly SoR dump.MariaDB 3306 (grant-scoped); Forgejo SSH 2222
benzeneRabbitMQ broker (10.5.2.50:5672, vhost /solari), the provisioning store and the Ansible tree.AMQP exchanges sor.events and notify.events; /srv/solari-provision/{store,ansible}
lithiumStatus-panel host. The solariPanel daemon drives the RP2350 LED panel over USB-CDC.Calls /api/panel*; serial protocol to the panel
radiumSamba AD DC (BIND9_DLZ for akoria.org) and Keycloak (sso.akoria.org:8443, realm akoria).OIDC issuer; AD DNS, written by sor_apply_ad INFERRED. That unit is not installed on xenon, and its unit file runs as root from /opt/solari-sorsync.
chlorineInternal CA (step-ca) at 10.7.0.10, and an authoritative BIND secondary ([dns.secondary.chlorine]).ACME / X.509 issuance used by the PKI routes; DNS
steelAuthoritative BIND secondary ([dns.secondary.steel]), applied through STACK_APPLY.DNS; one of the /api/dns/query targets

sor-apply-pihole is installed on xenon but disabled. Versions and units were read from the live hosts on 2026-11-03: SELECT VERSION() on both databases, php -v on xenon, the release RELEASE.json, and systemctl list-unit-files on xenon.

1.2Interface inventory

These are the interfaces an integrator or extender can build against. They are listed by stability: earlier rows are safer contracts.

InterfaceTransportConsumersContract ownerChapter
Dashboard REST APIHTTPS + JSON envelope, cookie sessionSPA, panel daemon, scriptsdashboard/api/ + docs/authz/AUTHZ_MANIFEST.json2, 3
CDC eventsAMQP topic sor.eventsDNS, AD and Pi-hole appliersmigrations 022–030 + deploy/sorsync/5
NotificationsAMQP topic notify.eventsnotifyd sendersdeploy/notify/9
SoR (read)MariaDB SQLreports, reconcilersnetdb/sor/6
Panel HTTP + serialHTTPS + USB-CDC frameslithium daemon, RP2350 firmwarestatus-panel/CONTRACT*.md7
ctl socketAF_UNIX, line protocolPHP API, solariCtlClientsrc/server/solariCtl.c4
SCPNNG over mutual TLS, binary TLVsolariClient, solariMonitorinclude/solari/solariFrame.h4
Provisioning helpersqueued ctl verbs → Python → Ansibledashboard Provisioning screensdeploy/fleet/8
Rule Integrations go through the REST API, or through read-only SoR SELECTs under a dedicated grant. Writing SoR tables directly bypasses validation, audit stamping and the CDC guarantees. Calling the ctl socket directly bypasses the HTTP policy layer. Neither is a supported integration path.

1.3Two databases, one direction of truth

The monitoring DB (xenon, MariaDB 11.8.6, migrations in db/migrations/) holds telemetry and fleet state: hostCurrent/hostHistory, probeCurrent, alertEvent, discovered, enrolment and the ctl request queue. It is written by solariServer and the API.

The SoR (cesium, MariaDB 12.3.2, netdb/sor/schema.sql plus netdb/sor/migrations/) holds intended state: entities, addressing, DNS, provisioning catalogue, inventory and RackWire plans. Changes to its CDC tables leave an outbox row in the same transaction, and downstream systems (DNS, AD) are rendered from it. Discovery flows the other way, through sor_reconcile_discovery, which promotes adopted monitoring rows into the SoR.

The two databases have independent migration numbering. Monitoring 033 and SoR 033 are unrelated files. Always say which stream you mean.

2REST fundamentals

The dashboard API is one PHP front controller under /api/. Every route returns the same JSON envelope and is guarded by the same default-deny policy. Learn the rules in this chapter once and they apply to all 194 routes.

2.1Base URL, transport and dispatch

Base URL
https://dashboard.akoria.net/api/ (Apache 2.4 on xenon, HTTPS only, HSTS)
Front controller
dashboard/api/index.php. It registers the auth routes, then routes.php (reads), then routes_mutations.php (writes). Each file loads routes/*.php group files from its $groups list.
Session gate
Every path except /api/auth/*, POST /api/enrollments and POST /api/enrollments/ack needs a session before dispatch.
Router
dashboard/api/lib/Router.php. Patterns use {name} segments. An unknown path returns 404 not_found. A known path with the wrong method returns 405 method_not_allowed.
Guard
Policy::enforce runs before every handler (dashboard/api/lib/Policy.php).

Every JSON response carries Content-Type: application/json; charset=utf-8, Cache-Control: no-store and X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff. Apache adds X-Frame-Options: SAMEORIGIN, Referrer-Policy: no-referrer and HSTS; these were observed live on 2026-11-03.

2.2Authentication

A session is an opaque PHP session cookie. Credentials are never held client-side. There are two ways to obtain a session.

Keycloak OIDC (primary)

The dashboard is a confidential client using the authorization-code flow. Live, the client is solarinet-dashboard in realm akoria at https://sso.akoria.org:8443/realms/akoria, with scopes openid profile email. Keycloak federates to the Samba AD on radium.

  1. GET /api/auth/oidc/login stores a fresh state and nonce in the session (valid for 600 s), then redirects with 302 to the Keycloak authorization endpoint.
  2. Keycloak authenticates the user and redirects to GET /api/auth/oidc/callback?code=…&state=….
  3. The API checks state and exchanges the code at the token endpoint. It then validates the ID token: signature via JWKS (RS256/384/512), iss, aud, exp and nonce. TLS to the IdP is verified against oidc.caBundle.
  4. Groups map to a role. oidc.adminGroups (default ["domain admins"]) gives admin. oidc.operatorGroups gives operator. Everyone else gets oidc.defaultRole (default viewer). The session ID is regenerated and the browser is sent to postLoginRedirect.
  5. On later requests the role is refreshed from HTTPS UserInfo at most every 60 s. If the UserInfo subject changes, the session is revoked.

Local password login (break-glass)

POST /api/auth/login with {"username","password"}. It is enabled only when SOLARI_LOCAL_LOGIN=1 and SOLARI_AUTH_RATE_DIR names an absolute, non-symlink, writable directory with mode 0700 or tighter. Attempts are limited to 5 per account and source in a rolling 15 minutes. Accounts live in the credential file at SOLARI_AUTH_FILE (default: project-root solari-auth.json). Hashes are verified with password_verify. Use dashboard/api/tools/solari-passwd.php to set one. On every request the local account is reloaded from that file, so a deleted or downgraded account loses access at once.

Session properties

PropertyValueSource
Cookiesolari_sess; HttpOnly, SameSite=Lax, Path=/, Secure when served over TLS, browser-session lifetimeAuth::bootSession
Idle timeout1800 sAuth::IDLE_MAX_SEC
Absolute lifetime28 800 s (8 h) local; 3600 s (1 h) OIDCABSOLUTE_MAX_SEC, OIDC_MAX_SEC
Rolesviewer < operator < admin; any other value ends the sessionAuth::current
FixationSession ID regenerated on every loginsession_regenerate_id(true)
PrincipalGET /api/auth/whoami → {operator, role, displayName, source, directoryEnabled, oidcEnabled, csrfToken}routes/auth.php
Directory bind Auth::directoryAuthenticate (direct LDAP/AD password bind) is a non-functional stub and always returns null. AD users sign in through Keycloak.

2.3Authorization policy

Policy::enforce is default-deny. A route may declare a policy as the fourth argument to $router->get/post/add:

$router->post('/api/example', static function (): void { … }, ['auth' => 'operator']);
$router->add('PUT', '/api/example/{id}', $handler, Policy::INVENTORY_CATALOG_WRITE);
$router->post('/api/panel/state', $handler, ['auth' => 'session', 'csrf' => 'panel-service']);
Declared authMeaningLive count
(undeclared)GET/HEAD/OPTIONS → session; every other method → operator—
publicNo session. Login, auth config, the two OIDC legs and the two enrolment-bearer routes. The bearer token is validated by the C bridge.6
sessionAny principal, including viewer86
operatoroperator or admin, else 403 forbidden102
adminadmin only (no route currently declares it)0
anything elseFails closed with 403 forbidden ("Unrecognized policy")—

The declared policy for every route is mirrored in docs/authz/AUTHZ_MANIFEST.json (phpRoutes[]: method, pattern, csrf, roleRequired, optional servicePrincipal). CI test tests/dashboard/test_authz_manifest.php fails when code and manifest disagree.

2.4CSRF

Every state-changing request authenticated by cookie must pass two checks, unless the route uses the panel-service bridge. Failing either returns 403 forbidden.

  1. Origin. The Origin header (or, failing that, the origin of Referer) must equal this request's own scheme://host or an entry in SOLARI_ALLOWED_ORIGINS (comma-separated). A request carrying neither header is rejected.
  2. Token. X-Solari-CSRF must equal the session's synchronizer token. Get the token from csrfToken in the POST /api/auth/login or GET /api/auth/whoami response. It lasts as long as the session.

Panel-service bridge. On routes declared 'csrf' => 'panel-service' (only POST /api/panel/state and POST /api/panel/config), CSRF is skipped only when both conditions hold:

  • the principal is the local account panel with role viewer;
  • the request has no Origin, Referer or Sec-Fetch-Site header, so it cannot come from a browser.

Use this pattern for any future headless service principal; do not loosen it.

Scripts A non-browser client making writes must send an Origin header matching the dashboard origin, as well as the cookie and X-Solari-CSRF. See §11.9 for a complete curl session.

2.5Response envelope

// success (HTTP 2xx)
{"ok": true,
 "data": { … },
 "ts": "2026-11-03T21:19:10Z"}
// failure (HTTP 4xx/5xx)
{"ok": false,
 "error": {"code": "conflict",
           "message": "Location changed since it was read."}}
  • ts is server time, ISO-8601 UTC with a Z suffix (Response::nowIso8601Utc).
  • Stored datetimes are emitted through Coerce::iso() as ISO-8601 Z. Large identifiers go through Coerce::id() and may arrive as strings; treat IDs as opaque.
  • Field names in data are camelCase on monitoring routes. Many SoR-backed routes (inventory, provisioning, RackWire) return SoR column names in snake_case. Each route card in §3 gives the actual shape.
  • Branch on error.code, which is stable. error.message is for humans and may change.
  • Two routes return non-JSON bodies on success: GET /api/inventory/codes/{id}/label (PNG or PDF bytes) and the OIDC redirects (302).

2.6Common errors

These apply to every route and are not repeated on each route card. Route-specific codes are listed on the cards and indexed in Appendix A.

HTTPerror.codeRaised whenOrigin
400bad_requestBody is not a JSON object or array; a path ID is malformed; a field fails validationsolari_json_body, route validators
401unauthorizedNo session, or the session expired (idle, absolute, revoked)session gate, Auth::requireSession
403forbiddenRole too low; CSRF origin or token failed; ctl peer refused a verbPolicy, Operator, SolariCtl
404not_foundNo such route, or no such rowRouter, handlers
405method_not_allowedPath exists, method does notRouter
409conflictOptimistic-lock mismatch; unique/FK violation (SQLSTATE 23000); SoR trigger rejection (SQLSTATE 45000, trigger message passed through)Inv::exec, ProvCatalog::dbFail, rw_write
413payload_too_largeBody exceeds a route's declared limitsolari_json_body($max)
415unsupported_media_typeNon-empty body whose type is not application/json (MIME parameters allowed)solari_json_body
500internal_errorUncaught exception, including an unmapped DB error. It is logged server-side.bootstrap.php
502control_unavailable / control_errorctl socket unreachable, or the bridge returned no request IDSolariCtl
503service_unavailable / busySoR unreachable or not configured; lock wait or deadlock on a provisioning or RackWire writeSor::db, ProvCatalog, rw_*
Since 037 Inventory writes that hit a MariaDB deadlock return HTTP 409 with a message asking the client to try again. They no longer surface as an unmapped 500. Retry the whole request with a fresh read; it is safe because the failed transaction rolled back. Provisioning and RackWire keep their existing 503 busy mapping.

2.7Request bodies and headers

  • JSON only. solari_json_body($maxBytes = null). An empty body is treated as {}. A non-empty body must be application/json and decode to an object or array.
  • Size limits apply only where a route passes one. Provisioning catalogue writes use ProvCatalog::body, which allows 262 144 bytes by default or 16 KiB on smaller routes. It also rejects unknown keys.
  • Path IDs must be positive decimal integers. Inventory validates them with Inv::idParam; provisioning accepts up to 4 294 967 295.
  • Idempotency-Key (optional) is accepted on queued control routes. When present it must be 8–128 characters from [A-Za-z0-9._-], otherwise 400. It is passed to REQUEST_SUBMIT, so a retried request maps to the same queued job.
  • Methods. Many fleet mutations are POST …/{id}/delete or POST …/{id}/remove rather than DELETE. Inventory and provisioning use PUT and DELETE. Use exactly the method in the catalogue.

2.8Pagination

There are three pagination styles; each route card says which applies.

StyleRoutesParametersResponse
Page envelopeInventory lists: components, models, receipts, projects, allocations, locations?flat=1limit 1–5000, offset 0–2 147 483 647. Supplying either enables paging; the other then defaults to limit=500 or offset=0.data = {items:[…], page:{limit, offset, total, hasMore, nextOffset}}
Catalogue pageGET /api/inventory/product-typeslimit 1–500 (default 100), offsetSame {items, page} shape
Clamped limitNode and gear history; code lists and historylimit clamped: history default 1000, max 5000. Code list default 500, max 5000. Code history default 100, max 1000.Plain array

Compatibility mode. If you omit both limit and offset on a page-envelope route, data is a bare array, exactly as before paging existed. Components still cap that array at 500 rows; the other lists are unbounded in this mode. New integrations should always send ?limit=500&offset=0 explicitly and loop while page.hasMore, using page.nextOffset.

GET /api/inventory/models?hw_type=server&limit=100&offset=200
→ {"ok":true,"data":{"items":[{"id":123,"make":"Example","model":"Unit"}],
   "page":{"limit":100,"offset":200,"total":237,"hasMore":false,"nextOffset":null}},
   "ts":"2026-10-21T00:00:00Z"}
  • total counts rows matching the filters, before paging. Order within each list is deterministic.
  • Offset pages are not a snapshot; inserts and deletes between requests shift them. Treat a changed total or a duplicate ID across pages as a failed read and retry. The dashboard's browser adapter does this. It reads 1000-row pages and refuses collections over 100 000 rows. Same-count field edits between pages are not detected.
  • The combined GET /api/inventory returns {models, units, locations, receipts, projects, allocations} only while every collection has 500 rows or fewer. Above that it returns 413 inventory_page_required. It takes no page parameters, so use the per-collection routes instead.

2.9Optimistic locking

Routes that edit rows people may edit at the same time use compare-on-write. Read the row, keep its version marker, and send the marker back with the edit.

SurfaceMarkerRequired?On mismatch
PUT /api/inventory/locations/{id}updated_at in the body: the raw DB value, or the ISO updatedAt from the GETYes409 conflict "Location changed since it was read."
PUT /api/inventory/container-types/{id}updated_atYes409 conflict
PUT /api/inventory/product-types/{id}updated_atOptional; checked only when sent409 conflict "Product type changed since it was read."
PUT/DELETE /api/inventory/components/{id}none—Last writer wins. Adding a check is on the backlog (§13).
RackWire plan update and restoreexpectedHash: a hash over name, thumbnail, notes and plan JSONYes409 plan_conflict, unless the requested state is already applied
Location mergePreview fingerprint (SHA-256) from merge-previewYes409 merge_conflict
Provisioning catalogue PUTsnone. These use SELECT … FOR UPDATE inside a transaction, so the last writer wins.——

loc_same_timestamp(current, expected) accepts either form of the timestamp, so clients may echo exactly what they read. On a 409, re-read, re-apply the user's change, and resubmit. Never retry blindly with the new marker.

Since 037 Edit-conflict timestamps carry microseconds. Two edits within the same second are no longer mistaken for one version. Clients must echo updated_at/updatedAt exactly as received; truncating to whole seconds will cause spurious 409s.

2.10Queued control operations

Privileged fleet actions (provision, decommission, deploy, discovery scans, pool and asset deletes, config pushes, provisioning helpers) cannot be executed by the web server's UID. The route enqueues them on the C server's queue (§4.2) and waits briefly.

  • SolariCtl::callQueued(verb, args, operator, idem, waitSec = 4.0) submits the job, then polls every 100 ms until waitSec runs out. POST /api/nodes/{id}/config waits 15 s.
  • If the job finishes in time, the response is the route's normal success.
  • If not, the route returns success with "status":"queued" and "request":"<id>". The route's own status field (for example "status":"provisioning") may be replaced or accompanied by request; see the card.
  • The only generic HTTP poll today is GET /api/discovery/scan-requests/{requestId}, which is scoped to discovery. For other queued jobs, re-read the affected resource.
  • Errors from the inner verb map to 403 forbidden, 502 control_unavailable or 400 control_error.

2.11Handler implementation rules

Anyone writing or reviewing a route must know these server-side rules.

  • Native prepares. Both Db (monitoring) and Sor (SoR) connect with PDO::ATTR_EMULATE_PREPARES => false. A named placeholder must not appear twice in one statement. MariaDB raises HY093, which surfaces as 500 internal_error. Use :from1/:from2, or positional ?. CI test tests/dashboard/test_pdo_placeholders.php scans every SQL literal under dashboard/api for this.
  • Prepared statements only. Db::rows/row/scalar/exec and the Inv::, ProvCatalog:: and rw_ wrappers all prepare and bind. Identifiers such as metric columns are chosen from whitelists, never interpolated.
  • SoR provenance. SoR writes stamp source_id (Sor::dashboardSourceId(), or the rackwire source), asserted_kind='human' and asserted_at=NOW(). Deletes are soft (deleted_at), and every read filters deleted_at IS NULL.
  • Monitoring writes. These go through the ctl socket, not direct SQL. The exceptions are alert acknowledgement, maintenance windows, push subscriptions and panel state, which write their own tables.

3Interface catalogue

This chapter lists every HTTP route the dashboard API registers: 194 routes at a28bdf2, grouped by domain. Each card is generated from the router source and the authorization manifest. Type in the search box, or use the chips below, to narrow the list.

3.1Overview

DomainRoutesReadWrite
Authentication & session642
Monitoring & overview24186
Status panel413
Fleet control, discovery & enrollment401228
DNS & network allocation871
Identity, PKI & Forgejo330
Provisioning351223
Inventory622636
RackWire1266
Total19489105

How to read a card.

  • The header shows the method, the path, and the role required. A CSRF badge means a state change needs Origin plus X-Solari-CSRF (§2.4).
  • Query and Body list accepted fields; * marks a required field.
  • Errors lists route-specific failures. The common errors in §2.6 are not repeated.
  • ctl verb names the privileged C-server verb the route invokes (§4).
  • Tables lists the monitoring or SoR tables the route reads or writes.

Paths, roles and CSRF flags were extracted mechanically and cross-checked against docs/authz/AUTHZ_MANIFEST.json; all 194 match. Body, response and error details were read from handler source by a second extraction pass, then spot-checked by hand. Small omissions are possible; the handler named in each card's source line is authoritative.

3.2Authentication & session · 6 routes

These six routes establish and inspect a session. /api/auth/config tells a client which login methods are enabled. /api/auth/whoami returns the principal and the session's csrfToken, which every write must echo (§2.4). The OIDC pair runs the Keycloak authorization-code flow; local login is gated by SOLARI_LOCAL_LOGIN (§2.2).

POST/api/auth/loginpublic

Authenticates a local username and password and returns the principal and CSRF token.

Body
JSON object
  • username string — Defaults to empty string.
  • password string — Defaults to empty string.
Success
200 · {operator,role,displayName,source,csrfToken}
Errors
  • 403 local_login_disabled — Local login is disabled.
  • 401 invalid_credentials — Credentials do not authenticate.
  • 415 unsupported_media_type — Nonempty body is not application/json.
  • 400 bad_request — Nonempty body is not a JSON object.
Notes
No explicit body-size limit. Authentication details are delegated to Auth.
POST /api/auth/login {"username":"operator","password":"fake-password"}
→ {"ok":true,"data":{"operator":"operator","role":"operator","displayName":"Operator","source":"local","csrfToken":"fake"},"ts":"2026-11-03T00:00:00Z"}

3.3Monitoring & overview · 24 routes

These are the read surface of the monitoring database: nodes, network gear, probes, alerts, rules, segments, topology and the summary roll-up. Writes are limited to alert acknowledgement, maintenance windows and Web Push subscriptions. History routes clamp limit (§2.8). GET /api/stream is a retired event-stream endpoint that now answers 204 at once. Poll the summary instead.

3.4Status panel · 4 routes

These four routes form the contract between the dashboard and the LED status panel daemon. The panel principal is a dedicated local account with deliberately narrow rights (§7.2). The binary frame format the daemon sends to the device is in §7.3.

GET/api/panelsession

Builds the panel dashboard snapshot, including systems, pools, alerts, panel state, commands, and screen configuration where available.

Success
200 · Panel snapshot payload assembled from system, pool, alert, probe, gear, panel-state, command, and screen-config queries.
Errors
Common errors only (§2.6).
Tables
node, asset, hostCurrent, probeTarget, probeCurrent, alertEvent, alertRule, pool, panelState, panelCommand, networkGear, gearInterfaceCurrent, panelScreenConfig
Notes
This is a read-only consistent-snapshot transaction except that service principals trigger expiry of pending commands older than 120 seconds. Full response includes additional keys defined in the handler.
GET /api/panel
→ {"ok":true,"data":{"systems":[],"pools":[],"alerts":{"info":0,"warn":0,"crit":0}},"ts":"2026-11-03T00:00:00Z"}

Queues a validated physical-panel command for the panel service to consume.

  • arg integer 0–255 required — Per kind: 1 ≤3; 2 ≤2; 3 ≤100; 4/5 ignored; 6 ∈ {3,6,30}; 7 ≤1; 8 (screenIdx<<1)|enabled ≤23; 9 (screenIdx<<3)|weightCode, screenIdx ≤11, weightCode ≤5.

3.5Fleet control, discovery & enrollment · 40 routes

Fleet routes cover the asset registry, discovery and adoption, enrolment approval, node configuration, and the control actions under /api/control/. Deploy, provision, fleet-provision, fleet-image, decommission and retire go through the queued REQUEST_SUBMIT path (§4.2). They answer 200 with a status, a log handle and, if the call outlasts the synchronous wait, a request id to poll. Survey and criticality changes are direct, synchronous verbs.

Discovery · 7
GET/api/discoverysession

Lists discovered devices for a selected status and decorates each with a best-effort topology neighbor and, when available, an inactive-asset tombstone.

Query
  • status string default new — Allowed values: new, ignored, adopting, adopted, all; anything else returns bad_request (400).
Success
200 · [{discId,host,ip,kind,via,services,segId,arch,mac,vendor,osName,deviceRole,sysDescr,enrichedAt,mdnsName,mdnsServices,osGuess,openPorts,banners,nmapEnrichedAt,seenCount,firstSeenAt,lastSeenAt,status,neighbor,tombstone?}]
Errors
  • 400 bad_request — status is outside the listed values.
Tables
discovered, networkGear, lldpEdge, node, asset, information_schema.COLUMNS
Notes
Ordered by lastSeenAt descending; tombstone is omitted unless a matching inactive asset exists, and neighbor is null when no host cross-reference exists.

POST/api/discovery/scanoperatorCSRF: Origin + X-Solari-CSRF

Submits an operator-requested bounded IPv4 discovery scan and returns either an admission-pending request id or a queued scan id.

Body
JSON object
  • cidr string required — Must be an IPv4 CIDR wholly inside RFC1918, loopback, or link-local space, with prefix 1–32 and at most 4096 usable hosts.
  • ports string — Comma-separated 1–64 unique decimal TCP ports, 1–65535; empty string uses the server default port set.

Errors

  • 400 control_bad_request — Framed operator request exceeds the bridge request limit.
  • 502 control_error — Bridge omits a request id or scan id, or a queued admission result is malformed.
  • 502 control_unavailable — Operator bridge transport is unreachable, incomplete, or cannot accept the full request.
  • 403 forbidden — The operator bridge denies authorization for the queued operation.

POST/api/discovery/{discId}/adoptoperatorCSRF: Origin + X-Solari-CSRF

Adopts a discovered candidate through the bridge and mirrors its IP/name into SoR when the discovery row exists.

Path
  • discId positive integer — Digits only and not zero; invalid values return 400 bad_request.
Body
JSON object
  • displayName string — Nonempty strings are truncated to 120 bytes and sent as name.
  • class string — Accepted values: server, appliance, network, iot, host, other; other values are ignored.
  • poolId integer — Integer or digit string; 0 means no pool, and a nonzero missing pool returns 400 bad_request.
  • notes string — Nonempty strings are truncated to 240 bytes.
  • tags array — Array is JSON-encoded and forwarded.
  • heartbeat boolean — Defaults true; any falsey value disables it.

Errors

  • 400 control_error — bridge rejects ADOPT
  • 403 forbidden — bridge refuses authorization
  • 502 control_unavailable — bridge transport fails

3.6DNS & network allocation · 8 routes

These routes are read-only views of the DNS release state (zones, records, hosts, RPZ, health, and a query probe). The one write approves or revokes a network's static-address allocator eligibility in the SoR. DNS content itself changes only through the SoR and CDC (§5). There is no API for editing records directly.

GET/api/networks/allocator-eligibilitysession

Lists live SoR networks with their live subnets, DHCP dynamic pools, IPv4 subnet counts, and overlaps against other networks.

Success
200 · [{id,name,purpose,enabled,management,is_default,allocator_eligible,allocator_approved_by,allocator_approved_at,vlan_tag,vlan_name,subnets:[{id,network_id,cidr,gateway_ip,dhcp_pools,ipv4}],ipv4_subnet_count,overlapping_networks,ipv4_overlap_count}]
Errors
  • 503 service_unavailable — A network or subnet read throws.
  • 503 service_unavailable — SoR is unconfigured or unreachable.
Tables
networks, vlans, subnets, dhcp_ranges
Notes
Only networks and subnets with deleted_at IS NULL are selected; overlapping_networks contains distinct other-network name/CIDR strings.
GET /api/networks/allocator-eligibility
→ {"ok":true,"data":[{"id":7,"name":"Servers","enabled":true,"allocator_eligible":true,"subnets":[{"id":12,"network_id":7,"cidr":"10.0.0.0/24","gateway_ip":"10.0.0.1","dhcp_pools":"10.0.0.100–10.0.0.180","ipv4":true}],"ipv4_subnet_count":1,"overlapping_networks":[],"ipv4_overlap_count":0}],"ts":"2026-11-03T12:00:00Z"}
Source: dashboard/api/routes/network_allocator.php:12

  • confirm boolean required — Must be true; otherwise confirmation_required (400).
Success
200 · {id,allocator_eligible}
Errors
  • 400 bad_request — Invalid id or eligible is missing/not boolean.
  • 400 confirmation_required — confirm is not exactly true.
  • 404 not_found — The network is absent or deleted.
  • 409 conflict — Approval is requested for a disabled network, a network without exactly one live IPv4 subnet, or an overlapping live IPv4 prefix.
  • 503 service_unavailable — SoR is unconfigured/unreachable or the transactional write fails.
  • 413 payload_too_large — A configured JSON body limit is exceeded.
  • 415 unsupported_media_type — A non-empty body is not application/json.
Tables
networks, subnets
Locking
Pessimistic row lock: SELECT ... FOR UPDATE on networks.id; no optimistic-lock field or 409 version code.
Notes
Approval writes allocator_approved_by and allocator_approved_at; revocation clears both. Body read with solari_json_body() and no explicit byte cap (only the PHP/Apache request limits apply).
POST /api/networks/7/allocator-eligibility with {"eligible":true,"confirm":true}
→ {"ok":true,"data":{"id":7,"allocator_eligible":true},"ts":"2026-11-03T12:00:00Z"}
Source: dashboard/api/routes/network_allocator.php:84
GET/api/dns/querysession

Queries the requested DNS name and record type concurrently against selected configured resolvers.

Query
  • name hostname/FQDN required — Must match the route's 1–253 character hostname pattern.
  • type string default A — Case-insensitive; accepted types are A, AAAA, CNAME, PTR, SRV, TXT, MX, NS, SOA, CAA, ANY.
  • servers comma-separated server ids default steel,xenon,radium,external — Blank also selects all; each nonblank id must be configured.
Success
200 · {name,type,ts,results:[{server:{id,label,ip,kind},status,flags,answers,authority,queryTimeMs,rpzBlocked,error}]}
Errors
  • 400 bad_request — Invalid name, type, or server id.
Notes
Resolver IPs come from the server-side registry; dig failures are represented in each result as status/error rather than route errors.

POST/api/provisioning/imagesoperatorCSRF: Origin + X-Solari-CSRF

Creates a provisioning image and returns the created row.

Body
JSON object
  • distro string required — Lowercase slug, up to 32 characters.
  • family string required — One of suse, debian, fedora.
  • release_name string required — Safe release token up to 48 characters.
  • release_label string|null — UTF-8, at most 96 characters.
  • arch string required — One of x86_64, arm64, armhf, riscv64, i386.
  • boot_method string required — One of ipxe, pi_netboot, media; pi_netboot requires arm64 or armhf.
  • source_url string|null — HTTP(S) URL, no whitespace/control characters, at most 512 bytes.
  • checksum_url string|null — Same URL validation as source_url.
  • install_template string|null — Safe template token, up to 128 characters.
  • refresh_policy string — If supplied, manual, weekly, or point_release.
  • netboot_verified boolean|0|1 — Boolean or integer 0/1.
  • notes string|null — UTF-8, at most 255 characters.
Success
201 · Image object with the standard image keys.
Errors
  • 400 bad_request — Invalid JSON shape, unknown field, missing required field, managed field supplied, or field validation fails.
  • 409 conflict — Duplicate key, FK, or catalog rule rejects insert.
  • 503 busy — Catalog lock timeout or deadlock occurs.
  • 500 db_error — Other DB error or created row cannot be read.
Tables
prov_images

POST/api/provisioning/image-refreshoperatorCSRF: Origin + X-Solari-CSRF

Queues an image refresh request for an existing image.

Body
JSON object
  • image positive integer ID required — Integer or digit string through 4294967295.
Success
200 · {image,status,log,request?}; status is queued or started.
Errors
  • 400 bad_request — Invalid body, image ID, or Idempotency-Key.
  • 404 not_found — Image does not exist.
  • 413 payload_too_large — JSON body exceeds 4096 bytes.
  • 415 unsupported_media_type — A nonempty body is not application/json.
  • 502 control_error — Queued ctl request fails or bridge returns no request ID.
  • 403 forbidden — Queued ctl rejects operator authorization.
  • 503 control_error — Ctl reports active-server/lease availability failure.
  • 409 conflict — Catalog constraint rejects a related DB operation.
  • 503 busy — Catalog deadlock or lock timeout.

3.9Inventory · 62 routes

Inventory is the largest domain. It is backed by the SoR on cesium; a route answers 503 when the SoR is unreachable. It covers product types and barcodes (the catalogue), items (components, stored as hardware_units), legacy models, locations and container types, receipts, projects and allocations, and system codes and labels. This section folds in and supersedes docs/SolariNet_Inventory_API.html rev. 1.4.

Paging
Components, models, receipts, projects, allocations and locations?flat=1 use the page envelope (§2.8). Omitting both limit and offset keeps the old bare-array shape.
Legacy aggregate
GET /api/inventory keeps its object shape (models, units, locations, receipts, projects, allocations) only while every collection has at most 500 rows. Beyond that it returns 413 inventory_page_required. It takes no page parameters, so large inventories must use the collection routes.
Catalogue
A product type says what something is; every physical or software instance is a separate item. Kinds: hardware, peripheral, component, software, accessory, consumable, other. Names are trimmed and required. Any key inside specs that looks like a secret is rejected at any depth: password, token, API key, licence key, credential, private key. Licence data is a non-secret reference only.
Barcodes
Codes are upper-cased with whitespace removed, and unique while live. A declared gtin must have a valid length and check digit. GET /api/inventory/product-barcodes?code= is an exact lookup that returns a prefill candidate. It never creates anything and never calls an external service. Duplicates return 409 duplicate_barcode.
Locations
Levels are property → floor → room → section, and a root must be a property. A storage_unit may sit under any level, including another storage unit. Live sibling names are unique, including among roots; soft-deleted rows release their name. The database enforces levels and rejects cycles. PUT refuses parent_id; use /move. Merge is two-step: merge-preview returns reference counts and a SHA-256 fingerprint, then merge takes that fingerprint and rechecks under lock. Repeating a completed merge returns the same result. Bulk storage-unit creation takes 1–500 units atomically.
Location errors
409 with one of duplicate_location, location_cycle, merge_conflict, container_not_empty or conflict (stale updated_at). A merge fingerprint expires when either endpoint or any referenced row changes.
Reading consistently
Page requests are not one database transaction. A changed page.total or a duplicate id across pages means the data moved; restart the read. The dashboard does this at 1000 rows per page and refuses collections over 100 000 rows.
Since 037 Add from the catalogue. Items can be created directly from a product type: choose the type, a quantity and a location, and the API creates that many items in one step. Such items carry product_type_id and may have a null model_id (§6). Software licences. Software-kind items record seats, expiry, a reference-only pointer to where the licence lives (never a key or licence text), and the machines it is installed on. Retired types are hidden from the Components picker. Location codes are unique across all live locations, not only among siblings; a duplicate returns 409.

POST/api/inventory/componentsoperatorCSRF: Origin + X-Solari-CSRF

Creates one item, or count identical items all-or-none, of a catalogue product type.

Body
JSON object
  • product_type_id integer required — A live (not retired) product type. Required unless model_id is sent.
  • model_id integer|null — Legacy hardware model. Alone, it derives the product type; with product_type_id, the two must agree.
  • count integer — 1–50. Creates that many rows in one transaction. When sent, the response is {items,count}.
  • serial string — Trimmed, maximum 128 bytes. Only with count 1; unique per product type.
  • asset_tag string — Trimmed, maximum 64 bytes. Only with count 1.
  • location_id integer|null — Must identify a live location.
  • acquired_on date|null — YYYY-MM-DD.
  • quantity positive integer — Defaults to 1.
  • unit_cost_cents non-negative integer|null — Integer cents.
  • stock_status enum — Defaults to in_stock.
  • receipt_id integer|null — Must identify a live receipt.
  • notes string|null — Trimmed.
  • licence_seats positive integer|null — Software items only; 1–4294967295.
  • licence_expires_on date|null — Software items only; a real YYYY-MM-DD date.
  • licence_reference string|null — Software items only; where the licence is kept (vault item, order number, URL), at most 1024 bytes. Values that look like a key, password or token are rejected.
  • installed_on_unit_id integer|null — Software items only; another live, non-software item.
Success
201 · component row; {items:[row…],count} when count is sent
Errors
  • 400 bad_request — No product type, retired type, model/type disagreement, count out of range, serial or asset tag with count > 1, licence fields on a non-software item, or any field invalid.
  • 409 conflict — Trigger SIGNAL, duplicate serial, constraint violation, or a deadlock/lock timeout ("try again"; safe to retry).
Tables
hardware_units, product_types, hardware_models, locations, receipts
Notes
Create stamps dashboard source and human assertion. model_id may be null on the stored row.

POST/api/inventory/locations/{id}/mergeoperatorCSRF: Origin + X-Solari-CSRF

Merges a source location into a target, retargeting supported references and soft-deleting the source.

Path
  • id positive integer — Source location.
Body
JSON object
  • target_id positive integer required — Must differ from source.
  • fingerprint string required — 64 lowercase hexadecimal characters from preview.
Success
200 · {source_id,target_id,merged,idempotent,counts,moved}
Errors
  • 400 bad_request — Target missing/invalid, fingerprint malformed, or IDs equal.
  • 404 not_found — Source or target is unavailable, except completed repeat merge.
  • 409 merge_conflict — Unsupported FK, stale fingerprint, type/project mismatch, or child collision.
  • 409 location_cycle — Target lies in source subtree.
  • 409 duplicate_location — Constraint reports duplicate.
  • 409 conflict — Related database constraint prevents change.

3.10RackWire · 12 routes

RackWire routes store rack and cable plans with version history, soft delete and restore, and a hardware library with port groups. POST /api/rackwire/commit resolves a plan's racks and cables into SoR locations and interconnects. It reports what was created, updated, skipped and committed; rerunning it is safe. Telemetry is a bounded key/value upload, truncated to RW_TELEMETRY_MAX_KEYS (256) keys.

GET/api/rackwire/planssession

Lists live plans as summaries without their plan JSON.

Success
200 · [{id,name,slug,updatedAt,createdAt}]
Errors
Common errors only (§2.6).
Tables
rw_plans
Notes
Includes all live plans ordered by updated_at descending; no pagination.

→ {"ok":true,"data":[{"id":12,"name":"Core rack","slug":"core-rack","updatedAt":"2026-11-01T12:00:00Z","createdAt":"2026-10-30T12:00:00Z"}],"ts":"2026-11-03T00:00:00Z"}

POST/api/rackwire/plansoperatorCSRF: Origin + X-Solari-CSRF

Creates a plan or updates an existing RackWire-owned plan.

Body
JSON object
  • string string required — Trimmed, nonempty, at most 128 bytes.
  • plan object required — Must decode as an array/object; serialized as JSON.
  • id positive integer — When supplied selects update; invalid values return bad_request/400.
  • expectedHash 64-character hexadecimal string — Required for updates; otherwise plan_hash_required/409.
  • thumbnail string or null — If omitted on update, preserves stored value; empty or nonstring becomes null.
  • notes string or null — If omitted on update, preserves stored value; empty or nonstring becomes null.
Success
201 · {id,name,slug,plan,planHash,thumbnail,notes,updatedAt,createdAt}
Errors
  • 400 bad_request — invalid id, missing/invalid name, missing/nonobject plan, or plan JSON serialization failure
  • 404 not_found — update target is absent or no longer live
  • 409 plan_hash_required — update lacks valid expectedHash
  • 409 conflict — target belongs to another source or a constraint is violated
  • 409 plan_conflict — expectedHash differs and requested state is not already applied
  • 503 busy — row lock times out or deadlocks
  • 503 service_unavailable — SoR unavailable or RackWire source is missing
  • 415 unsupported_media_type — nonempty body lacks application/json
  • 400 bad_request — nonempty body is not a JSON object
Tables
rw_plans, sources
Locking
Update locks the plan row; expectedHash is checked against name, thumbnail, notes, and plan JSON. Mismatch returns plan_conflict/409 unless the requested state is already present.
Notes
Updates preserve thumbnail/notes only when their keys are omitted; create and update return 201. Device and cable counts are derived from plan.devices and plan.cables.
POST /api/rackwire/plans with {"name":"Core rack","plan":{"devices":[],"cables":[]}}
→ {"ok":true,"data":{"id":12,"name":"Core rack","slug":"core-rack","plan":{"devices":[],"cables":[]},"planHash":"aaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaa","thumbnail":null,"notes":null,"updatedAt":"2026-11-03T00:00:00Z","createdAt":"2026-11-03T00:00:00Z"},"ts":"2026-11-03T00:00:00Z"}

  • 503 service_unavailable — SoR unavailable or RackWire source is missing
  • 415 unsupported_media_type — nonempty body lacks application/json
  • 400 bad_request — nonempty body is not a JSON object
Tables
rw_plans, rw_plan_versions, sources
Locking
Locks the plan row and compares expectedHash; mismatch returns plan_conflict/409 unless the snapshot JSON is already current.
Notes
Restore changes plan_json only; name, slug, thumbnail, notes, and denormalized counts are not updated by this handler.

4Control socket & SCP wire protocol

Two binary-adjacent interfaces sit underneath the REST API. solariCtl is a local, line-oriented Unix socket. The dashboard uses it to ask the C server to act. SCP (SolariNet Control Protocol) is the mutual-TLS framed protocol between the server and every client and monitor agent. Neither is reachable from the network for integrators: build on the REST API (§2–3) and treat this chapter as the contract you must keep when you change the C tier.

4.1solariCtl: socket and framing

Socket
/run/solari/solariCtl.sock (AF_UNIX). Configured by the server key bind.server.ctlSocket, with group from ctl.socketGid, mode 0660 (best effort). PHP reads the path from SOLARI_CTL_SOCK.
Framing
One request per connection: VERB key=value key=value\n. The reply is OK [key=value …]\n or ERR <solariStatus> <message>\n. Values containing spaces must be URL-encoded.
Authorization
SO_PEERCRED UID, never anything in the request. Root or the configured operator UID → operator (may call every verb). The configured dashboard UID (the PHP-FPM pool user) → dashboard (ordinary verbs plus the queue). Any other UID → unknown, denied.
Fail-closed default
The C allowlist names the dashboard-ordinary verbs explicitly (src/server/solariCtl.c). A new verb is privileged until it is added to that list.
Standby
On a standby server only PING, REQUEST_GET and DISCOVER_STATUS run.
PHP client
dashboard/api/lib/SolariCtl.php: send() (raw), call() (maps ERR to HTTP), enqueue(), poll(), callQueued(). The socket timeout is 5 s.
$ printf 'PING\n' | sudo socat - UNIX-CONNECT:/run/solari/solariCtl.sock
OK pong decommissionInline=1

This exchange was observed live on xenon on 2026-11-03. The socket there was srw-rw---- jason:solari-ctl. Operators can also use /opt/solarinet/current/bin/solariCtlClient. Run privileged verbs by hand only when following a runbook.

4.2The privileged-work queue

The web server must never hold privilege. So the dashboard UID may not call privileged verbs directly; it submits them:

  1. REQUEST_SUBMIT verb=<PRIVILEGED_VERB> op=<operator> [idem=<key>] …args → OK request=<id>. Only privileged inner verbs are accepted, and op= is mandatory. The row records the peer UID and the optional idempotency key, so a repeated key returns the existing request.
  2. The server's queue worker claims the request and runs the inner verb with server privilege. The state moves pending → claimed → done | failed.
  3. REQUEST_GET request=<id> returns state and result, scoped to the requester.

The PHP wrapper callQueued() does all three and returns early with {request, status:"queued"} once its wait budget (4 s by default) runs out (§2.10). Destructive verbs such as DECOMMISSION add a one-time confirmation token, consumed when the queue runs. Queued decommission tokens are redacted at rest (monitoring migration 033).

4.3Verb table

Generated from ctlVerbs[] in docs/authz/AUTHZ_MANIFEST.json; there are 38 verbs.

  • Class is the peer-ACL class: ordinary, privileged, queue or public-enrollment.
  • Operator UID, Destructive and Standby are manifest metadata. The C ACL above is what enforces access.
  • PHP callers lists the routes that invoke each verb.

4.4SCP transport

Library
NNG 1.12 scalability protocols over TLS (Mbed TLS 3.6), wrapped in lib/solari/solariNet.c. Peer authentication is mandatory on both sides.
Server ports
7701 ingest (reports and ACKs) · 7702 survey and control (surveyor/respondent) · 7703 PUB (broadcast). Defaults are in src/server/serverContext.c.
Identity
The certificate CN is <role>.<fqdn>, where role is client, monitor or server. Ingest derives the role from the leading label, resolves it against the enrolment record, and rejects any HELLO whose identity disagrees with the certificate. Inbound message types are then checked against a per-role matrix (src/server/serverIngest.c).
Enrolment
Not an SCP message. A new agent POSTs a CSR to /api/enrollments using a 256-bit bearer token minted by ENROLL_TOKEN. The C bridge revalidates the token's digest, expiry, pre-bound host and role. An operator approves the request and the agent picks up its certificate with /api/enrollments/ack.

4.5SCP frame format

Every integer is big-endian. frameLen counts the header plus payload, excluding itself and the CRC. The CRC-32 covers header plus payload. The maximum body is 1 MiB. The protocol version is SCP_PROTO_VERSION = 1.

TypeValueDirectionPayload (TLVs)
HELLO0x01node → serverrole, agent version, host FQDN, OS, arch, config epoch
WELCOME0x02server → nodesession acknowledgement
CLIENT_REPORT0x10client → serverFQDN, OS/arch, per-core CPU, RAM/swap, disks, interfaces, USB, watched processes/logs, host-health counters
MONITOR_REPORT0x11monitor → serverFQDN, probe results, link stats
WATCHDOG0x12node → servernone; the header timestamp refreshes liveness
DISCOVERY_ADVERT0x13node → serversource, repeated ip|mac|iface entities, proto|port|banner services
TOPOLOGY_REPORT0x14node → serveruplink localIf|gatewayIp|speedMbps, LLDP chassis|port|localIf, segment ifName|cidr
PEER_ALIVE0x15monitor ↔ monitorliveness between monitors
SURVEY / SURVEY_RESP0x20/0x21server ↔ fleetthe response is parsed as a monitor report
CONTROL / CONTROL_RESULT0x30/0x31server ↔ nodecommand; result correlated by corrId
DECOMMISSION0x32server → nodewipe scope
WHO_IS_PRIMARY / PRIMARY_IS0x40/0x41node ↔ serverfailover lease query
ACK / ERROR0x50/0x51eitherERROR: uint16 code + detail string

The TLV format is uint16 type · uint16 length · value, and receivers skip unknown types. That is what makes additive changes safe: add a new TLV type rather than changing the meaning of an existing one, and older servers will ignore it. The TLV registry lives in include/solari/solariTlv.h; message structs are in include/solari/solariMsg.h.

Inferred CONTROL_RESULT, WHO_IS_PRIMARY and ACK have no dedicated payload struct in solariMsg.h. Their shapes are defined by the handlers that build and parse them. Read serverIngest.c and the client control path before changing them.

5Change data capture & messaging

SoR writes reach DNS, AD and other consumers through a transactional outbox, an emitter and RabbitMQ. If you want to react to SoR changes, add a consumer here; do not poll the SoR. Read deploy/sorsync/README.md together with this chapter.

5.1Outbox

Migration 022 created two tables; 023, 024, 026 and 030 added the triggers.

sor_outbox
idBIGINT auto-increment PK (the event's outbox_id)
table_namesource table
row_idnullable row PK
actioninsert | update | delete
changed_attimestamp
sor_sync_state
kPK: emit_checkpoint, discovery_checkpoint
vlast processed value
updated_attimestamp

Each CDC table has AFTER INSERT, AFTER UPDATE and AFTER DELETE triggers that each insert one outbox row in the same transaction as the change. A rolled-back write therefore emits nothing. Insert and update use NEW.id; delete uses OLD.id.

Live, 2026-11-03. There are 69 outbox triggers across 23 tables. The other 15 triggers in the schema are validation and compatibility triggers, such as the 033/035 inventory bridge.

dhcp_ranges dns_records dns_zones entities entity_functions external_refs functions
function_incompatibility interfaces ip_addresses networks prov_images prov_runs
prov_software prov_software_artifacts prov_software_conflicts prov_software_requires
prov_stacks prov_stack_deps prov_stack_members sources subnets vlans
Not in CDC Inventory tables (hardware_units, product_types, locations, …) and RackWire tables do not emit outbox events. A consumer that needs inventory changes must first add triggers in a ledgered migration (§11.4).

5.2Emitter (sor_emitd)

  • Polls sor_outbox WHERE id > emit_checkpoint ORDER BY id in batches and publishes each row to the durable topic exchange sor.events with publisher confirms.
  • Routing key sor.<table>.<action>, e.g. sor.dns_records.update. Messages use delivery mode 2 (persistent) and content_type=application/json, with no custom headers.
  • Publishing is mandatory: an unroutable event (no bound queue) blocks the checkpoint. The exception is tables listed in [amqp] optional_tables, which may be published with no consumer.
  • The checkpoint advances only after the whole batch is confirmed. Delivery is therefore at-least-once, and a consumer may see an event twice.
  • Unit sor-emit.service runs on xenon. Its config is SORSYNC_CONF (default: the sorsync.conf next to the script), sections [sor_emit]/[sor] and [amqp].

Event message

exchange:    sor.events   (topic, durable)
routing key: sor.ip_addresses.update
properties:  delivery_mode=2, content_type=application/json
body:
{"outbox_id": 160, "table": "ip_addresses", "row_id": 4211,
 "action": "update", "ts": 1762204750}

The event is a change signal, not a row image. ts is UNIX_TIMESTAMP(changed_at). A consumer re-reads current SoR state, usually rendering the whole artefact it owns, so duplicate, reordered or missed events all converge to the same result.

Observed The live checkpoint read emit_checkpoint=160 and MAX(sor_outbox.id)=160, so the emitter was caught up. The example above uses these values for illustration.

5.3Appliers

ApplierQueueBinding keysEffectStateLive
sor_apply_dnsdsor.apply.dnssor.{entities,ip_addresses,interfaces,dns_records,dns_zones,networks,subnets}.*Renders a full zone generation, releases it to the BIND primary on xenon and verifies it, then refreshes and SOA-verifies each [dns.secondary.<name>] (chlorine, steel)JSON target-state file ([dns] target_state_file)running on xenon

sor_reconcile_discoverynone—Reverse path. Polls monitoring discovered rows (status='adopted') and inserts the missing entities and IPs into the SoR with source solarinet_monitor. Its writes then flow through CDC.reads rows directly; discovery_checkpoint unusedrunning on xenon (sor-reconcile)

Delivery contract (deploy/sorsync/delivery.py)

  • consume_after_apply() sets prefetch, collects deliveries for debounce_sec, then calls the apply callback once for the batch.
  • Acks happen only if the callback returns exactly True. An exception or False leaves the batch unacked; closing the connection requeues it and the loop reconnects after reconnect_delay_sec.
  • Each applier reconciles once at start-up and every reconcile_interval_sec, which repairs anything missed while it was down.
  • There is no dead-letter exchange and no retry queue. A permanently failing apply keeps redelivering, so watch the unit's journal.
Backstop refresh solari-dns-secondary-refresh.service re-pushes the secondary zone set from xenon to the secondaries. It reads /etc/bind/solarinet-current/named.conf.managed plus /etc/bind/named.conf.rpz, and its path unit watches /etc/bind so a release symlink swap triggers it. (Before 2026-11-03 it read the retired named.conf.akoria fragments and failed closed; fixed in fbdb460.)

5.4Notification bus

A second exchange carries human-facing alerts (§9):

exchange:    notify.events   (topic, durable)
routing key: notify.<severity>         e.g. notify.critical
queue:       notify.dispatch  bound to notify.#
body:
{"severity":"critical","title":"cesium down","body":"no report for 300 s",
 "channel":"auto","to":null,"source":"alertbridge","ts":"2026-11-03T21:00:00Z"}

Only title or body is required. alertbridge publishes {title, body, severity, source}, and notifyd fills in defaults.

Broker The live emitter points at AMQP vhost solari on 10.5.2.50:5672 (RabbitMQ on benzene), according to the [amqp] url in xenon's sorsync.conf. The URL contains credentials and is referenced by path only. The broker's own state was not inspected. Mosquitto (MQTT) is a separate broker that only the mqtt notify sender uses.

6The SoR as a read surface

The System of Record is the MariaDB database sor on cesium (MariaDB 12.3.2). It is the canonical store for hosts, addressing, DNS, provisioning, inventory and RackWire. Reporting and integrations may read it directly. Every write goes through the REST API, because the API carries the provenance, locking and validation rules.

6.1Access rules

  1. Read-only, always. The only credential provisioned for tooling today is the broad application account solari, which has ALL on sor.* from 10.0.0.0/8. Jason accepted this on 2026-11-02. Nothing in the database stops a write, so read-only is a discipline you must keep. A dedicated SELECT-only reporting account is a backlog item (§13).
  2. Never copy credentials. Use the existing option file through a short-lived, owner-only copy, then shred it: 
    umask 077
sudo -n sed 's/^\[sor\]/[client]/' /etc/solarinet/prov-sor.cnf > /dev/shm/q.cnf
mariadb --defaults-file=/dev/shm/q.cnf -e "SELECT …"
shred -u /dev/shm/q.cnf
  3. Filter tombstones. Nearly every business table soft-deletes. Add WHERE deleted_at IS NULL unless you want history.
  4. Never read secrets. That table exists for legacy reasons. Licences and credentials are stored by reference only (remit decision 6); export nothing from it.
  5. Expect schema change. Every migration is announced in netdb/sor/migrations/. Views (v_*) are the most stable read targets.

6.2Data model at a glance

Live on 2026-11-03: 60 base tables and 4 views. Groupings follow netdb/sor/SCHEMA.md.

AreaTables
Identity & provenancesources (12 slugs: manual, dashboard, netdb, samba_ad, bind, forgejo, solarinet_monitor, unifi, pihole, rackwire, provisioner, scanner-ingest), audit_log, users, groups, group_members
