Provenance columns. Asserted rows carry source_id → sources.id, asserted_kind (human, discovered, …), asserted_at, created_at/updated_at, and deleted_at for soft delete. Filter by source to see who said what, e.g. rows the reconciler added with source_id = (SELECT id FROM sources WHERE slug='solarinet_monitor').

Since 037 hardware_units.model_id is nullable. An item created from a catalogue product type carries product_type_id and may have no legacy hardware_models row. Reports must join on product_type_id first and treat model_id as optional. Software items also carry licence seats, expiry, a reference-only licence pointer and an "installed on" link to a non-software unit. Location code is unique among live locations across the whole tree.

6.3IP addresses: the INET6 rule

Address columns (ip_addresses.address and others) use MariaDB's native INET6 type. IPv4 values are stored as v4-mapped (::ffff:10.0.0.1). The legacy string functions silently produce wrong answers:

Expression (live result on 2026-11-03)Use?
WHERE address = CAST('10.0.0.1' AS INET6) → 1 row✔ preferred
WHERE address = CAST('::ffff:10.0.0.1' AS INET6) → 1 row✔
WHERE address = INET6_ATON('::ffff:10.0.0.1') → 1 row✔ (v4-mapped literal only)
WHERE address = INET6_ATON('10.0.0.1') → 0 rows✘ silent miss
SELECT INET6_NTOA(address) → NULL✘ use CAST(address AS CHAR)

To display an address, take CAST(address AS CHAR) and strip a leading ::ffff: for IPv4.

6.4Useful read queries

-- live hosts with their primary IPv4 and OS
SELECT e.name, REPLACE(CAST(ip.address AS CHAR), '::ffff:', '') AS ipv4, os.name AS os
FROM entities e
LEFT JOIN ip_addresses ip ON ip.entity_id = e.id AND ip.deleted_at IS NULL
LEFT JOIN operating_systems os ON os.id = e.os_id
WHERE e.deleted_at IS NULL
ORDER BY e.name;

-- what DNS will publish (same view the DNS applier renders from)
SELECT * FROM v_dns_hosts ORDER BY 1 LIMIT 20;

-- migration head
SELECT migration_id, state, applied_at FROM solari_migration_ledger
WHERE migration_id <> 'schema' ORDER BY migration_id DESC LIMIT 1;

All three queries were run read-only against the live SoR on 2026-11-03. v_dns_hosts returns host, ip, role, lifecycle.

6.5The monitoring database (separate)

Live telemetry lives in a different database: MariaDB 11.8.6 on xenon, configured through SOLARI_DB_*. It holds hostCurrent/hostHistory, probeCurrent, alertEvent, discovered, networkGear, lldpEdge, enrolment and queue tables. It has its own migration stream in db/migrations/, whose numbers overlap the SoR's. For example, monitoring 037 (node_certcn_role_prefix) is unrelated to SoR 037. Read it through the API (§3, Monitoring) rather than directly; its schema changes more often.

7Status panel interface

The status panel is a Galactic Unicorn LED matrix (RP2350). It shows fleet health and sounds an alarm for critical episodes. Three contracts meet here: four REST routes under /api/panel, the solariPanel C daemon that bridges REST to the device, and a framed binary protocol over USB-CDC serial or a mutual-TLS socket. The authoritative wire definition is status-panel/protocol.h. This chapter summarises it, and the header wins if the two disagree.

The alarm test is loud. A critical alert or a tier-threshold breach arms the panel's audible alarm. Do not raise synthetic critical alerts to test an integration unless someone is beside the panel and ready to acknowledge it.

7.1The chain

  1. The dashboard composes a snapshot from systems, pools, alerts, probes, gear, the reported panel state, pending commands and the screen configuration: GET /api/panel.
  2. solariPanel (systemd solari-panel.service on lithium) logs in as the local principal panel. It polls every pollSec (default 5 s) and re-sends the latest snapshot to the device every snapshotSec (default 2 s). If the data is more than 35 s old at send time it sets dataStale.
  3. Frames go to the device over USB-CDC (serialDev, default glob /dev/serial/by-id/*, first match wins). They can also go over an optional raw-TLS listener for the standalone Wi-Fi panel. Both transports can run together.
  4. The device answers with STATE and CONFIG frames. The daemon relays them back through POST /api/panel/state and /api/panel/config. Operator commands queued by POST /api/panel/command ride down as CONTROL frames.

7.2REST contract

RoutePolicyBodyResponse
GET /api/panelsession—Snapshot payload (systems, pools, alerts, probes, gear, panelState, pending commands, screen config).
POST /api/panel/commandoperator + CSRF{kind, arg}{cmdId}. 409 queue_full when 16 or more commands are pending. 403 for the panel principal itself.
POST /api/panel/statesession, panel principal only, csrf: panel-serviceDevice STATE fields{panelState:{theme, screen, brightnessPct, autoBright, sleeping, alarmArmed, alarmAcked, dwellSec, ackedEpisodeId, lastCmdId, reportedAt}}. Commands up to lastCmdId are marked applied.
POST /api/panel/configsession, panel principal only, csrf: panel-serviceDevice CONFIG fields{panelScreenConfig:{screenCfg, flags, reportedAt} | null}

Command kinds

Every kind is idempotent on the device, so a re-sent command is harmless.

kindNamearg
1SETTHEME0–3
2SETSCREEN0–2, within the current theme
3BRIGHTNESS0–100; latches manual brightness
4AUTOBRIGHTignored; re-enables the light sensor
5ACKALARMignored; same path as the physical acknowledge button
6SETDWELLseconds, one of 3, 6, 30
7SLEEP0 wake, 1 sleep
8SCREENEN(screenIdx << 1) | enabled, ≤ 23
9SCREENWT(screenIdx << 3) | weightCode; screenIdx ≤ 11, weightCode ≤ 5

The panel service principal and the CSRF bridge

The daemon authenticates as the local account panel with role viewer. Its password is read from passFile. The daemon refuses to start unless that file is owned by the service user and has mode 0600.

The state and config routes declare ['csrf' => 'panel-service']. Policy::isCsrfBridged() skips the CSRF check only when two things are true. First, the principal is exactly source=local, username=panel, role=viewer. Second, the request has no Origin, Referer or Sec-Fetch-Site header. Any browser-originated request still gets the full check.

The bridge exists for deployed daemons that predate X-Solari-CSRF. The code comment says it should be removed once the daemon echoes the login csrfToken. See the roadmap in §13.

7.3Wire framing

Header
6 bytes: magic A5 53, version (0x01), type, then u16 payloadLen.
Trailer
2-byte CRC-16/CCITT (poly 0x1021, init 0xFFFF). It covers version, type, length and payload, but not the magic.
Parser
The parser scans byte by byte for the magic. It rejects an oversize length before buffering anything. A frame with a bad CRC is dropped, and scanning resumes inside it. Frames with an unknown type or version are skipped without losing sync. Snapshots with a duplicate or older seq are ignored; the comparison is RFC 1982 wraparound-aware.
Limits
Maximum payload 2048 bytes. A partial frame is abandoned after 500 ms.
Byte order
Little-endian throughout.
Host → panel
TypeNamePayload
0x01SNAPSHOTFixed layout, below
0x02PING—
0x03HELLOREQ—
0x04CONTROL8 B: u32 cmdId, u8 kind, u8 arg, 2 reserved
0x05PROVISIONTLV, 192-byte chunks
Panel → host
TypeNamePayload
0x81HELLOIdentity / version
0x82EVENTBUTTON 1, ACK 2 (+8-byte episode), THEMECHANGE 3, LINKLOST 4, LINKBACK 5
0x83LOG≤ 128 ASCII bytes
0x84STATE16 B
0x85CONFIG16 B, including u8[12] screenCfg
0x86PROVACK5 B

SNAPSHOT layout

BlockOffset / strideContents
globals0, 40 Bu32 ts, u16 seq (duplicates ignored), u16 score, alarmActive, dataStale, stateRoll[5] (ok, degraded, down, unknown, maint), alertCounts[3] (info, warn, crit), meanLoadPct, poolCount, systemCount, hasTopAlert, u32 rx/tx kbps, u16 rttTenthMs, u16 lossPermille, 6 reserved
pools40, stride 20, ≤ 8name char[8], tier 0–3, per-state counts, total, loadPct, 4 reserved
systemsafter pools, stride 16, ≤ 64name char[12], state, pool index (0xFF = unpooled), tier, loadPct
topAlertafter systems, 84 B, only if hasTopAlertu32 alertId, u32 episodeId, severity, 3 reserved, subject char[24], detail char[48]

States are OK 0, DEGRADED 1, DOWN 2, UNKNOWN 3 and MAINT 4. MAINT is reserved and is always 0 today. Severities are INFO 0, WARN 1 and CRIT 2. Names use the uppercase charset [A-Z0-9 .:-].

A new episodeId re-arms the tone, and acknowledged episodes are never reused. History such as load ribbons and RTT sparklines is not sent on the wire. The firmware builds it up from successive snapshots.

PROVISION limits

SSID ≤ 32 bytes, PSK 8–63, host ≤ 253, CA certificate ≤ 2048, client certificate ≤ 4096, client key ≤ 2048. These are used for the standalone Wi-Fi panel.

7.4Daemon configuration

The config file is /etc/solari-panel/solari-panel.conf. Each line is key=value, and # starts a comment. The template is status-panel/daemon/solari-panel.conf.example.

KeyMeaning
apiBaseDashboard base URL, e.g. https://dashboard.akoria.net (required)
user, passFilePanel principal and its password file (required; file mode 0600, owned by the service user)
pollSec, snapshotSecAPI poll interval (default 5 s) and frame resend interval (default 2 s); both must be positive
serialDevUSB-CDC device path or glob; set it empty for listener-only mode
listen, listenAddrOptional TLS listener port and address. Wildcard addresses are refused. If the address is omitted, the daemon uses the default-route address.
tlsCert, tlsKey, clientCaListener chain, key and client CA (e.g. chlorine root). Required when listen is set. Files must be owned by root or the daemon user; key mode 0600, certificate and CA 0644 or stricter.
allowedSanThe single client SAN accepted, e.g. panel-01.panel.akoria.net. The client certificate also needs the clientAuth EKU.
denylistFile of hex certificate serials, one per line, loaded at startup

The listener requires TLS 1.2 or later and uses an AEAD-only cipher list. Deployment, flashing and certificate issuance are covered by the panel runbooks in docs/panel/ and the firmware developer reference docs/panel/SolariNet_Panel_Developer_Reference.html. This manual does not repeat them.

7.5Changing the panel contract

  • Change protocol.h/protocol.c once. Both the daemon and the firmware compile it. Then rebuild both and refresh the golden and parity fixtures in status-panel/fixtures/.
  • Reserved bytes must be zero on send and ignored on receive. Prefer additive changes. The gear list is the model: a same-version trailer after the base SNAPSHOT layout, detected by presence, so older receivers ignore it. Bump the version byte only for a breaking change, because receivers skip frames whose version they do not know.
  • A new command kind needs four changes: the PanelControlKind enum, firmware handling, the argument validation in dashboard/api/routes/panel.php, and the panel tests.

8Provisioning & Ansible hooks

Provisioning turns SoR catalogue rows (images, software items, stacks) into running machines. The REST layer validates the request and queues a privileged ctl verb. The C server runs a Python helper. The helper reaches the provisioning host, benzene, over SSH and runs Ansible from a published tree there. The design record is docs/Provisioning_UI_Design.md, phases P1–P7.

8.1Catalogue API and validation

The routes are /api/provisioning/{images, software, stacks}, with sub-resources for artifacts, requires, conflicts, members and deps. Reads need a session and writes need operator (§3). Validation lives in dashboard/api/lib/ProvCatalog.php. It mirrors the CHECK constraints of migration 029 one for one, so a bad request returns a field-specific 400 instead of a database error.

A few rules are deliberately stricter than 029 for newly supplied values. Stored values are never re-validated, so seeded rows stay editable.

  • release_name, install_template and ansible_role must each be one safe path token.
  • Column URLs must be http(s) and form a single token.
  • Spec keys are limited to the sets deploy/fleet/stackgen.py accepts. Item types are pkg, repo, artifact, script, build and role. A repo spec needs at least one family block (suse, debian, fedora).
  • An empty applies_* list is refused. Use null to mean "all".
  • Regexes anchor with \z, never $, so a trailing newline is rejected.
Enumerations
Arches: x86_64, arm64, armhf, riscv64, i386 (artifacts may also use any). Boot methods: ipxe, pi_netboot, media. Refresh: manual, weekly, point_release. Run modes: reimage, apply, refresh. Run statuses: queued, running, succeeded, failed, cancelled.
DB error mapping
Duplicate key or FK → 409. CHECK → 400. SIGNAL → 409. Lock wait or deadlock (1205, 1213, 40001) → 503 busy. Anything else → 500. SQL text and driver messages are never returned.

8.2The three enqueue verbs

Route → verbBodyHelper (in the release)
POST /api/provisioning/image-refresh → IMAGE_REFRESH{image}deploy/fleet/image-refresh.py: fetches and verifies an image and updates the netboot menu
POST /api/provisioning/stage → PROV_STAGE{entity, image, stacks[], mac}deploy/fleet/prov-stage.py: stages a reimage plus first-boot stack work; prov-finish.py completes it on first boot
POST /api/provisioning/apply → STACK_APPLY{entity, stacks[], dry_run}deploy/fleet/stack-apply.py: applies SoR stacks to a running machine through Ansible

IDs match ^[1-9][0-9]{0,9}$ and must not exceed 4294967295. stacks holds 1–32 distinct slugs matching ^[a-z0-9][a-z0-9-]{0,47}$. dry_run must be a JSON boolean. The MAC is normalised to lowercase with colons.

Each route calls SolariCtl::callQueued() with the operator and the Idempotency-Key header. It returns status: "queued" or "started" plus a log handle, and a request id when the call outlasts the synchronous wait (§4.2). Track progress with GET /api/provisioning/runs[/{id}] and GET /api/provisioning/log?kind=image-refresh|prov-stage|stack-apply&id=N.

Helpers write <ctl socket dir>/<kind>-<id>.log with mode 0640, group-readable by the ctl group. The log route returns the last 64 KiB.

8.3The Ansible tree

Source
deploy/fleet/ansible/: ansible.cfg, playbooks/site.yml, inventory/hosts.yml (example only), group_vars/all.yml, and roles base-os, common-stack, solarinet-client, stack-items, dev-tools, directory-join, dns-secondary and shared-home. stack-items dispatches on item type, with one step-*.yml per type (artifact, build, pkg, repo, role, script) plus guard.yml.
Rendering
deploy/fleet/stackgen.py turns SoR stacks into Ansible variables. stack-apply.py writes a temporary vars file per run. Host-specific values live in inventory/host_vars/<host>.yml on benzene.
Where it runs
On benzene, from ${PROV_ROOT}/ansible (default PROV_ROOT=/srv/solari-provision). It never runs from the release on xenon. Helpers reach benzene as ${BENZENE} (default jason@benzene.akoria.net) with ssh -o BatchMode=yes.
Image store
${PROV_ROOT}/store is NAS-backed and guarded by prov-store-guard.sh. Store health is written to <ctl socket dir>/prov-store.json and served by GET /api/provisioning/store: {known, store, fstype, source, reachable, mounted, linked, ok, size_bytes, avail_bytes, reason, checked_at, stale}.

Publishing the tree: sync-ansible.sh

Every release that changes deploy/fleet/ansible must be published to benzene after install-release.py:

/opt/solarinet/current/deploy/fleet/sync-ansible.sh           # publish
/opt/solarinet/current/deploy/fleet/sync-ansible.sh --check   # diff only: 0 in sync, 1 drift, 2 check failed
  • Each publish extracts into its own directory under ${PROV_ROOT}/ansible.releases/. Directory names sort in publish order.
  • ${PROV_ROOT}/ansible is a symlink, switched with one atomic rename. A playbook that is already running keeps resolving files in the tree it started with.
  • The newest three trees are kept, and ansible.prev points at the previous one. To roll back, repoint the symlink.
  • benzene's own inventory/ is carried forward.
  • Each tree carries a .release marker holding the release version.
Inferred: the "(unknown)" stamp. The script takes the marker's value from RELEASE.json two levels above the script ("version"). When there is no such file, or the value fails ^[A-Za-z0-9._-]+$, it writes unknown. A git checkout has no RELEASE.json, but an installed release does: /opt/solarinet/current/RELEASE.json holds rc-20261103-c39f565-io. So an "(unknown)" stamp most likely means the publish ran from a checkout rather than from /opt/solarinet/current. The marker on benzene was not read for this manual.

8.4Extending provisioning

  1. Add the SoR shape: a migration (§11.3), plus CDC triggers if appliers must see it (030 is the model).
  2. Teach ProvCatalog the new fields and keys. Its SPEC_KEYS must match stackgen.py's *_KEYS exactly.
  3. Render it in stackgen.py, and add the role or task file under deploy/fleet/ansible/roles/.
  4. Add tests: tests/dashboard/test_provisioning_api.php (it uses the fake ctl client in _prov_worker.php, so no socket is needed) and tests/deploy/test_prov_helpers.py and its neighbours for the helpers.
  5. Release, then run sync-ansible.sh. Without that step the new role exists on xenon but not where playbooks run.

9Notifications

Notifications are carried on the message bus. Any process can publish a small JSON message to the RabbitMQ topic exchange notify.events. notifyd consumes it and fans it out to pluggable senders. alertbridge turns the C server's alertEvent rows into such messages, and it pages when a reporting node goes silent. Publishers never need to know which transport delivers the message.

9.1Message contract

Publish to exchange notify.events (durable topic, vhost solari) with any routing key under notify.. The queue is bound with notify.#, so every key reaches it. alertbridge uses notify.<severity>.

{
  "severity": "crit",                 // crit | warn | info (default info)
  "title": "xenon: disk /var 94% full",
  "body": "Free space on /var dropped below 10% …",
  "channel": "auto",                  // auto = route by severity, or a sender name
  "to": ["…"],                        // optional; else [defaults] default_<sender>_to
  "source": "monitoring",             // free-form provenance tag
  "ts": 1783483080                    // unix seconds; default = receipt time
}

At least one of title or body is required. Values that are supplied are validated. A message that cannot be parsed is logged and dropped.

For one-off use from a shell or an agent, run deploy/notify/notify-send.py --severity warn --title … --body … [--source …]. It reads the AMQP URL from notify.conf, or from --config.

9.2Dispatch and delivery semantics

  1. Choose senders. If channel names an enabled sender, only that sender runs. If it names an unknown or disabled sender, a warning is logged and routing falls back to severity. Otherwise [routing] <severity> lists the senders. If that is empty, [defaults] default_channel is used.
  2. Resolve recipients. The message's to wins. Otherwise the sender's default_sms_to, default_push_to, default_apple_to or default_email_to is used.
  3. Send. Each sender receives message plus _to and _routing_key, and its own [sender.<name>] section.
  4. Acknowledge. The message is acked if any sender succeeded. It is also acked, after an error is logged, if all senders failed. There is no requeue and no dead-letter exchange, so delivery is at most once per sender. A TTL or DLX on the broker is the documented path if replay is ever wanted.

9.3Senders

Config nameModuleStateTransport
logsenders/log.pyworkingAppends a line to /var/log/solarinet/notify.log and logs to journald. This is the durable fallback.
applesenders/apple.pyworkingSSH to an always-on Mac and send an iMessage with osascript. iCloud then fans it out to every Apple device.
emailsenders/mail.pyimplementediCloud SMTP from a dedicated address, using an app-specific password held in notify.conf. The file is named mail.py so it does not shadow the stdlib email module.
webpushsenders/webpush.pyimplementedWeb Push to installed dashboards. Subscriptions are read from the monitoring DB, and messages are signed with a VAPID key file.
mqttsenders/mqtt.pyimplementedRepublishes to Mosquitto under notify/<severity> for the Tab5 display.
smssenders/sms_tachyon.pystubTachyon cellular SBC. The send_sms() seam has two candidate backends (mmcli_ssh, AT), and both only log what they would run.
pushsenders/push.pystubInterface-only placeholder, so routing that names push does not crash.

The "State" column comes from each module's own docstring. On 2026-11-03, notify.service and alertbridge.service were both active on xenon. notifyd runs as /opt/solarinet/current/apps/notify/notifyd.py --config …/deploy/notify/notify.conf, so its config path is still in a checkout rather than /etc.

The Proton mail migration means the iCloud-based email and apple paths may change. See §13.

9.4alertbridge: bridge and dead-man's switch

Bridge
Polls the monitoring DB with alertEvent WHERE eventId > checkpoint, joined to node and alertRule. It publishes each new row to notify.events as notify.<severity>.
Dead-man's switch
A node that was reporting is declared silent after max(120 s, 3 × sample_interval_sec). The client default for sampleIntervalSec is 15. A silent node raises a crit notification.
Config
Path from ALERTBRIDGE_CONF. Sections [db], [amqp] (url, exchange, reconnect_delay_sec), [bridge] (poll_interval_sec, batch, source), [deadman] (enabled, sample_interval_sec) and [state] (file; defaults to alertbridge.state.json next to the config, holding the checkpoint and the silent set). The DB credentials come from an environment file that the unit sources.
Contract
docs/design/HOST_HEALTH_CONTRACT.md §6 (Unit D); operator view in docs/SolariNet_Host_Health_Monitoring.md.

9.5notify.conf reference

The template is deploy/notify/notify.conf.example. Real files are gitignored. Never commit them.

SectionKeys
[amqp]url, exchange (notify.events), queue (notify.dispatch), binding_keys (notify.#), reconnect_delay_sec
[defaults]default_sms_to, default_push_to, default_apple_to, default_email_to, default_channel
[routing]crit, warn, info, each a comma-separated sender list
[senders]enabled. Unknown names are logged and skipped, never fatal.
[sender.log]path
[sender.sms]method, host, ssh_user, ssh_key, modem_index, at_device, at_baud

[sender.webpush]db_host, db_port, db_name, db_user, db_pass, vapid_private_key, vapid_subject

The procedure for adding a sender is recipe §11.8.

10Configuration reference

This chapter lists key and variable names, never values. Every file named here has a checked-in .example or .sample template. The real files are gitignored, or live outside the repo with restrictive modes. Never commit or paste their contents.

Live paths are not yet /etc-clean. On xenon (2026-11-03) several units still read config from a working checkout rather than /etc/solarinet:

The hardened template expects /etc/solarinet/server.conf. The binaries do run from the immutable release. Moving the configs is a follow-up (§13).

11Extension recipes

Each recipe lists, in order, every file a change of that kind must touch, the test that proves it, and the trap most likely to catch you. They assume the contracts in §2–§10. The map below shows where each recipe lands.

Common rules
  • Core daemons are standard C99 (CMAKE_C_EXTENSIONS OFF). The dashboard back end is PHP. Ops glue is Python under deploy/.
  • No server-side JavaScript, and no build step or bundler. The dashboard's JSX is compiled in the browser (§11.1).
  • Every SoR schema change is a ledgered migration with a rollback script and a rehearsal (§11.3).
  • Every new route or ctl verb gets an AUTHZ_MANIFEST.json entry. CI fails if the manifest and the code disagree.

11.1Add a dashboard screen

The dashboard is a static single-page app in dashboard/public/. index.html loads each source as <script type="application/x-solarinet-jsx" src="X.jsx?v=…">. boot.js compiles those scripts in the browser with the vendored Babel 7.29.0 and caches the output in Cache Storage under solari-jsx-transform-v2. Nothing is compiled ahead of time.

  1. Write the component. Put it in a new screensN.jsx (the current files run screens.jsx to screens8.jsx) or in the screen file for that area. Export it on window at the end of the file, for example Object.assign(window, { ProvImagesScreen }) (screens-prov.jsx:2205). There are no modules or imports; globals are the linkage.
  2. Load it. Add a <script type="application/x-solarinet-jsx" src="screensN.jsx?v=…"> tag to index.html after the files it depends on (api.jsx, components.jsx, data.jsx). Bump the ?v= on every file you change, because it is the cache key.
  3. Precache it. Add the file to the precache list in sw.js and bump CACHE_VERSION (currently "solari-v15"). If you skip this, offline and iPad home-screen users keep the old bundle.
  4. Route it. In app.jsx, add the component to the destructure from window (line 8). Add a render line next to the others (around line 291): {route.name === "x" && XScreen && <XScreen toast={toast} onNavigate={go} />}. Add a command-palette entry near line 227.
  5. Navigate to it. Add a nav item to components.jsx (around line 185), for example { id: "x", label: "…", icon: "…" }. Icons come from icons.jsx.
  6. Declare its maturity. Map the route to a capability in data.jsx (lines 643–657). test_capability_maturity.js fails on a route with no mapping.
  7. Fetch data only through window.SolariAPI (api.jsx). Add the endpoint to the EP map (from line 262) and call getJSON/post. unwrap() captures csrfToken and unwraps the {ok,data} envelope. Never call fetch directly; you would lose the CSRF header and error mapping.
  8. Test. Run node tests/dashboard/test_jsx_parse.js, test_layout.js, test_capability_maturity.js and any screen-specific test_*_ui.js. CI runs these with Node (ci.yml lines 723–738). Node is a CI tool only; nothing in the repo depends on it at runtime or build time.
Trap A JSX syntax error does not fail any build, because there is none. The screen silently fails to register in the browser. test_jsx_parse.js is the only gate, so run it before pushing. Design and test for iPad Safari first.

11.2Add an API route

  1. Choose the file. SoR-backed CRUD goes in a file under dashboard/api/routes/. Each file returns static function (Router $router): void { … }. Bridges to solariCtl verbs go in routes_mutations.php.
  2. Register the file in the $groups array in routes.php. The loader calls $register = require $file; $register($router); in array order. Order matters where files share helpers: inv_codes.php and location_admin.php must load after inventory.php.
  3. Declare the policy as the fourth argument: $router->post('/api/x', $handler, ['auth' => 'operator']). The values are public | session | operator | admin. With no declaration, GET/HEAD/OPTIONS default to session and every write defaults to operator. Writes also get the Origin check and X-Solari-CSRF check automatically (§2.4). Do not declare csrf: 'panel-service' on a new route; that bridge is scheduled for removal (§13).
  4. Write the handler with the shared helpers:
    • Bodies: solari_json_body($max).
    • Output: Response::ok($data, $status) and Response::error($code, $message, $status). These produce the envelope in §2.
    • Normalising values: Coerce::{iso,json,int,float,bool,id}.
    • Re-checking the role inside a handler: Operator::requireOperator().
    Map database errors to named codes and never return SQL text. For optimistic locking, copy the expected_updated_at pattern (§2.9).
  5. Bind each placeholder once. PDO runs with native prepares, so a repeated :name raises HY093, which surfaces as a 500. Use :a1, :a2, and so on. tests/dashboard/test_pdo_placeholders.php fails CI on a reuse.
  6. Declare constants above the file-scope return. Constants are not hoisted, so a const written below the return never runs. See rackwire.php:83-99, which declares RW_TELEMETRY_MAX_KEYS before the return on line 100.
  7. Update the manifest. Add a phpRoutes entry to docs/authz/AUTHZ_MANIFEST.json, for example {"method":"POST","pattern":"/api/x","csrf":"origin+session-token","roleRequired":"operator"}. Reads use "csrf":"none". In the baseline, 100 entries use origin+session-token, 92 use none and 2 use panel-service. A route that calls a ctl verb must also be listed in that verb's phpCallers under ctlVerbs.
  8. Queue privileged verbs. A verb whose class is privileged must be called through callQueued (REQUEST_SUBMIT, §4), never directly. test_authz_manifest.php fails if a privileged verb is called directly or privilegedDirectCallSites is non-empty.
  9. Test. Run php tests/dashboard/test_authz_manifest.php and test_pdo_placeholders.php, plus a route test modelled on the nearest tests/dashboard/test_*.php for the same area.
  10. Document it. Appendix B is generated from the router table when this manual is rebuilt, so the new route appears there automatically. The description in §3 and any new error codes in §2 are written by hand; add them.

11.3Add a SoR migration

  1. Create netdb/sor/migrations/NNN_name.sql with the next free three-digit number. tools/migrate.py --database sor applies files in order and records each one in solari_migration_ledger, with a SHA-256 checksum. Never edit an applied file: the checksum check will refuse the whole stream.
  2. Write the reverse in netdb/sor/rollback/NNN-down.sql and mention it in the migration's header comment. The runner has no rollback command; rollback scripts are applied by hand or in rehearsal. If the rollback needs data the forward step destroys, snapshot that data into a table inside the migration. 036 does this with _solarinet_m036_location_baseline.
  3. Add a rehearsal script, tests/netdb/test_<name>_NNN.sh, modelled on test_prov_images_os_034.sh. It applies forward, checks the result, rolls back, and applies forward again against a scratch database. It must refuse to run unless the target is a disposable database. In CI it runs with SOLARI_TEST_SOR_DB=1 and MYSQL_HOST=127.0.0.1.
  4. Add the rehearsal as a step in both CI workflows, Forgejo and GitHub.
  5. Regenerate the schema snapshot with tools/export_schema.py into netdb/sor/schema.snapshot.sql, and run tools/audit_schema.py. Commit the snapshot. migrate.py --verify-live compares the live schema against it.
  6. If the new table should emit CDC events, add its trigger in the same migration and follow §11.4 step 5.
  7. Add grants for any new runtime identity in deploy/sorsync/sor-runtime-grants.sql.
INET6 SoR IP columns are native INET6. INET6_NTOA(col) and a bare INET6_ATON('10.0.0.1') silently return the wrong value against that type. Use CAST(col AS CHAR) and CAST('…' AS INET6), or the v4-mapped ::ffff: form (§6).

Applying a migration in production, and backing out of it, is covered in §12.3.

11.4Add a CDC applier

An applier consumes the sor.events topic exchange and makes some external system match the SoR (§5).

  1. Write the consumer as deploy/sorsync/sor_apply_<name>.py. It loads config through db_config.py, connects to the broker, and declares a durable queue bound to sor.events with the keys it cares about, for example sor.dns_hosts.*. It reconciles once from current SoR state, then consumes through delivery.consume_after_apply(). A message is acknowledged only when the apply callback returns exactly True. Copy sor_apply_dnsd.py:276.
  2. Write the unit as deploy/sorsync/sor-apply-<name>.service, following sor-apply-dns.service for restart policy and config path.
  3. Grant least privilege in sor-runtime-grants.sql: SELECT on exactly the tables the applier renders.
  4. Test with unit and behaviour tests in deploy/sorsync/test_*.py. CI's sorsync job discovers them automatically and runs them against a disposable MariaDB and RabbitMQ.
  5. Coordinate optional_tables. The emitter publishes as mandatory unless a table appears in [amqp] optional_tables. An unroutable mandatory message stalls the checkpoint. For a new CDC table: deploy the trigger with the table in the optional list, then start the consumer, and only then remove the table from the list (see the hazard note in 030).
Design rule Treat the event as a hint. Apply the current SoR state, idempotently, for the whole artefact you own. Duplicate, reordered and missed events then all converge. A periodic reconcile catches anything missed.

11.5Add a client metric (C)

  1. Collect it in the PAL. Declare the function in src/client/platOS.h and implement it in src/client/plat/platLinux.c, plus every other plat/<os>.c that exists. Exactly one plat file is compiled. PAL functions return solariStatus, write into caller-owned buffers, and never allocate. An OS that cannot supply the value returns a "not supported" status rather than inventing a value.
  2. Carry it. Add the field to solariClientReport in include/solari/solariMsg.h. Assign a TLV id in include/solari/solariTlv.h from the host-metrics range 0x10xx. 0x1001–0x100D and 0x1010–0x1014 are taken, so check the header for the next free id. All integers are big-endian.
  3. Encode and decode in lib/solari/solariMsg.c, with the writer alongside TLV_CR_HEALTH_SCALARS (line 133) and the reader case near line 251. Fill the field in src/client/clientCollect.c and clientReport.c.
  4. Persist it in src/server/serverDb.c (prototypes are in server.h). Put the column in a new monitoring migration, db/migrations/NNN_name.sql. That stream is separate from the SoR and currently ends at 037. Apply it with tools/migrate.py --database monitoring.
  5. Test with Unity suites in tests/unit/: extend test_msg.c (round trip), test_tlv.c, test_platlinux.c and test_server_db.c. New suites are added to the list in tests/CMakeLists.txt and run under CTest.
Compatibility Readers skip unknown TLVs. Old servers therefore accept reports from new clients, and new servers must accept reports with the TLV missing: default the field and store NULL. Never change the meaning or width of an existing TLV; add a new id instead. This is what lets the protocol grow without a version bump (§4).

11.6Add an SNMP or discovery source

There are two layers. Monitoring-side discovery fills the monitoring database's discovered table. The reverse reconciler, deploy/sorsync/sor_reconcile_discovery.py, then proposes or merges those rows into the SoR.

  • Client-observed source (seen from a host). Add a value to the TLV_DISC_SOURCE enum (0x1203: 1 mDNS, 2 ARP, 3 advert, 4 portscan, 5 LLDP). Emit it from src/client/clientDiscovery.c and handle it in src/server/serverDiscovery.c and serverDiscoveryEnrich.c. Test with test_server_discovery*.c.
  • SNMP data. Build with -DSOLARI_WITH_SNMP=ON. The code shells out to net-snmp's snmpbulkwalk at runtime; there is no libnetsnmp build dependency. Interface counters belong in serverSnmp.c, run by the standalone solariSnmpPoll (systemd-timer friendly, kept out of the failover-critical server loop). LLDP neighbours belong in serverTopology.c. Test with test_server_snmp.c and test_server_topo_snmp.c.
  • Out-of-band enricher (Python). Copy the pattern in deploy/discovery/: a <name>.py, a <name>.conf.example and a solari-<name>.service. The enricher UPDATEs existing discovered rows by IP and never overwrites via, the first-discovery method. The existing enrichers are mdns_inspect.py, avahi_import.py, oui_enrich.py and nmap_enrich.py.
  • Provenance in the SoR. SoR rows record who asserted them through source_id → sources and asserted_kind. A new automated source gets its own sources slug through a migration (§11.3), and writes asserted_kind='machine'.
Scope Active scanning is capped at about 4096 hosts, a /20, per discovery scan. Keep new active sources inside the private ranges the existing enrichers use.

11.7Add an inventory product kind or location level

Both lists are closed enumerations enforced in three places: the SoR ENUM, PHP validation, and the UI. Change all three in one change set.

LayerProduct kindLocation level
SoRproduct_types.kind ENUM (033): hardware, peripheral, component, software, accessory, consumable, otherlocations.loc_type ENUM (036): property, floor, room, section, storage_unit, plus triggers trg_locations_level_ins and trg_locations_level_upd
PHProutes/product_catalog.php, lines 19 and 157 (inline lists)routes/location_admin.php: LOC_CHILD_LEVEL (property → floor → room → section)
UIscreens4.jsx: CAT_KINDS and CAT_KIND_LABEL (line 1587)screens8.jsx: LOC_ICON and TAG_LOC_TYPES (line 53)
  1. Write a migration (§11.3) that widens the ENUM. When renaming or removing a value, follow 036's widen → remap → narrow sequence. Update the level triggers if the hierarchy changes.
  2. Update the PHP list and the hierarchy map, then the UI constants and labels.
  3. For a new location level, decide whether it can hold storage units. A storage_unit may sit under anything, including another storage unit.
  4. Test with the inventory PHP route tests and test_inventory_*_ui.js.
Prefer data to enum If the new "kind" is really a sort of container, add a row to container_types through the UI or API. It is an editable table, and no migration is needed.
Since 037 The Components picker hides retired (soft-deleted) product types, so retiring a type needs no picker change. Software-kind types carry licence fields: seats, expiry, a reference-only licence pointer (never the key itself), and installed-on links. A new kind that needs per-item fields should follow that pattern rather than overloading notes. Live location codes are globally unique, so a new level's code scheme must not collide with existing codes.

11.8Add a notification channel

  1. Create deploy/notify/senders/<name>.py exporting send(message, cfg) -> bool. message is the decoded event plus _to and _routing_key. cfg is that sender's [sender.<name>] section. Return False on an expected failure rather than raising.
  2. Add it to REGISTRY in notifyd.py and to [senders] enabled.
  3. Add a [sender.<name>] block to notify.conf.example, with keys only and no values. Reference secrets by file path, not inline.
  4. Route to it with [routing] <severity> = …, or let a publisher pick it explicitly with channel (§9.2).
  5. Test with deploy/notify/test_notifyd.py (dispatch, routing) and test_notify_send.py (CLI).
Delivery guarantee notifyd acknowledges a message even when every sender fails. There is no requeue or dead-letter queue, so a channel that must not lose alerts needs its own retry and spool (§9.2, §13).

11.9Drive the API from an external tool

A script uses the same session, Origin and CSRF rules as the browser. This complete curl session reads a paged list and makes one write. Run it as an operator account. The password comes from a file, never from the command line.

B=https://dashboard.akoria.net
J=$(mktemp); trap 'rm -f "$J"' EXIT
# 1. Log in (local accounts; needs SOLARI_LOCAL_LOGIN=1 on the server)
jq -n --arg u ops --rawfile p ~/.config/solari/ops.pass '{username:$u,password:($p|rtrimstr("\n"))}' |
  curl -sS -c "$J" -H 'Content-Type: application/json' -H "Origin: $B" --data @- "$B/api/auth/login" >/dev/null
# 2. Fetch the session's CSRF token
TOK=$(curl -sS -b "$J" "$B/api/auth/whoami" | jq -r .data.csrfToken)
# 3. Read every page
off=0
while :; do
  r=$(curl -sS -b "$J" "$B/api/inventory/components?limit=500&offset=$off")
  echo "$r" | jq -c '.data.items[]'
  [ "$(echo "$r" | jq .data.page.hasMore)" = true ] || break
  off=$(echo "$r" | jq .data.page.nextOffset)
done
# 4. Write: cookie + Origin + X-Solari-CSRF
curl -sS -b "$J" -H "Origin: $B" -H "X-Solari-CSRF: $TOK" -H 'Content-Type: application/json' \
  --data '{"code":"SN-000123"}' "$B/api/inventory/codes/lookup" | jq .
  • Check .ok on every response. On failure, branch on .error.code, never on the message text (Appendix A).
  • Writes without Origin are rejected with 403 forbidden even when the token is right.
  • SSO-only accounts cannot use this flow, because OIDC needs a browser. Create a dedicated local operator account for automation.
  • Retry 503 busy with back-off. On 409 (edit conflict), re-read the row first.
  • Prefer the SoR (§6, read-only) for bulk reporting. Use the API when you need its validation or business rules.
Inference The account name ops and the path ~/.config/solari/ops.pass are placeholders. The code-lookup body shape was read from inv_codes.php:84.

12Maintenance

This chapter covers what keeps the interfaces in §2–§10 stable over time: pinned dependencies, immutable releases, ledgered migrations with compensating rollbacks, backups, a proposed deprecation policy, and the test matrix that guards all of it.

12.1Dependencies

LayerDependencyVersion / pinWhere pinned
C coreMbed TLSmbedtls-3.6.7 (068ff08); framework submodule dde0c4aGit submodules under third_party/; third_party/README.md
NNGv1.12.5 (437a5f7)
cJSONv1.7.19 (c859b25)

  • Upgrading a C dependency. Move the submodule to a release tag, update the table in third_party/README.md, and rebuild the pinned I/O prefix with deploy/build-pinned-io-deps.sh. Release binaries link against that prefix (SOLARI_VENDORED_DEPS=OFF), so a submodule bump has no effect on releases until the prefix is rebuilt. Run the full CTest suite and the cross builds. Clones used for releases need --recursive.
  • Upgrading a vendored JS file. Replace the file, bump its ?v= and the sw.js CACHE_VERSION. If Babel changed, also bump the transform cache name in boot.js (solari-jsx-transform-v2) so stale compiled output is discarded.
  • Python. Change pins in deploy/release/requirements.txt; the next release installs them. Range-pinned components should move to exact pins (§13).

12.2Versioning and release

SolariNet has no semantic version. The runnable unit is an immutable release named rc-YYYYMMDD-<sha7>-io. The SHA is the source commit and -io marks a build with the I/O feature set. A milestone on main gets a dated tag, currently release-20261030.

  1. Build from a clean, committed checkout. deploy/build-release-binaries.sh <prefix> <build-dir> compiles against the pinned I/O prefix with -DSOLARI_WITH_IO=ON -DSOLARI_WITH_SQLITE=ON -DSOLARI_WITH_JSON=ON -DSOLARI_WITH_DISCOVERY_TOOLS=ON.
  2. Package with deploy/dashboard/package-release.py. It produces an allowlisted tarball holding the server, scan worker, ctl client, client, monitor and SNMP binaries; dashboard/api and public; the fleet and deploy helpers; and the Python daemon sources. It writes RELEASE.json with the version, sourceRevision, sourceTreeDirty, the RackWire submodule pin and a SHA-256 manifest. It refuses a dirty tree unless given --allow-dirty, and the installer then refuses that bundle without the same opt-in.
  3. Install with deploy/dashboard/install-release.py. It rejects unsafe paths, symlinks, undeclared files and hash mismatches. It stages the tree under /opt/solarinet/releases/<version>, builds .venv, and atomically switches /opt/solarinet/current. It does not restart services.
  4. Cut over with deploy/host-configs/xenon/cutover-immutable.sh, then check with verify-immutable-processes.py. Every daemon must run from /opt/solarinet/current. Procedure: docs/immutable-daemon-release-runbook.md.
  5. Publish Ansible to benzene with deploy/fleet/sync-ansible.sh, run from /opt/solarinet/current so the tree is stamped with the release version (§8.3).
  6. Roll back by re-activating a retained release. That is a symlink switch plus a service restart. Xenon held 35 retained releases on 2026-11-03; current pointed at rc-20261103-c39f565-io.
Contract versions The wire contracts version themselves independently of releases:
  • SCP uses TLV extensibility (§4), so no version bump is needed.
  • Panel frames carry PANEL_PROTO_VERSION = 1 and grow through additive, presence-detected trailers (§7.3).
  • The authz manifest has schemaVersion.
  • The SoR evolves through ledgered migrations (§12.3).
The REST API is not versioned; its stability rule is §12.5.

12.3Migrations and rollback

There are two ledgered streams. The monitoring stream lives in db/migrations/, currently ending at 037. The SoR stream is netdb/sor/schema.sql plus netdb/sor/migrations/; source and the live ledger both end at 037 (§6). Both are applied by tools/migrate.py. The runner keeps solari_migration_ledger(migration_id, checksum, state, started_at, applied_at) and refuses:

  • a changed checksum on any applied file;
  • an interrupted or out-of-order history;
  • a non-empty database it does not recognise.

Applying in production

  1. Confirm last night's backup exists (§12.4), or take a fresh dump of sor on cesium.
  2. tools/migrate.py --database sor --db-name sor --defaults-file <option file> --check lists what is pending. It is the non-applying mode; there is no --dry-run.
  3. Apply by running the same command without --check.
  4. Run --verify-live to compare the live schema with schema.snapshot.sql.
  5. Deploy the release whose code expects the new schema. Order matters: migrations are written to be additive first, so the old code keeps working against the new schema during the gap.

Backing out

The runner has no rollback command. Each migration has a compensating script, netdb/sor/rollback/NNN-down.sql (or a grouped one such as 024-028-down.sql), which lives outside migrations/ so the runner cannot discover it. The scripts are defensive. 036's rollback runs a guard procedure that SIGNALs and refuses if any location changed since the migration's baseline snapshot. It never deletes operator data. After a successful rollback, delete that migration's ledger row by hand; every script header says so. DDL auto-commits, so a rollback that fails halfway must be finished by hand, not re-run blindly.

Prefer forward fixes Once operators have written data that depends on a migration, the guarded rollbacks will (correctly) refuse. The normal recovery is then a new forward migration. Rollback scripts exist for the window between apply and first use, and for CI rehearsal (tests/netdb/test_sor_migration_rollback.sh).

12.4Backups

deploy/backups/solari-backup.sh runs from the templated solari-backup@<host>.timer nightly at 03:30 with jitter. Each host's config is /etc/solari-backup-<host>.conf.

HostWhatTarget
cesiumMariaDB sor, plus Forgejo once relocated/data/backups/ on a separate spindle (sdb1) from the live datadir
xenonMariaDB solarinet (monitoring)An off-host NAS target is documented but not yet provisioned (§13)
  • Safety. Before publishing a dump, the runner decodes the whole gzip stream and rejects an empty payload. The target directory must be root-owned with mode exactly 0700, and artefacts are 0600. Retention is 14 days, pruning only files that match the backup name pattern.
  • Failure alerting. On failure, solari-notify-failure@.service publishes to notify.events (§9).
  • Restore is documented in docs/SolariNet_Backup_and_Restore_Runbook_2026-10-20.html.
Observed On xenon, solari-backup@xenon.timer last ran at 2026-11-03 03:31 UTC and was next due 2026-11-04 03:38 UTC. The cesium timer was not checked from xenon.

What backups do not cover. Configuration under /etc/solarinet, the live configs in the xenon checkout (§10), and benzene's provisioning store are not in the nightly dump. Releases are reproducible from git.

12.5Deprecation policy PROPOSED

No written policy exists today. The rules below are proposed. They codify what the code already does and are for the Lead and operator to adopt or amend.

  1. Additive by default. New response fields, new optional request fields, new routes, new TLVs and new panel trailers do not need notice. Clients must ignore unknown fields. The code already does this for TLVs and panel frames.
  2. Breaking changes need a compatibility window. A breaking change is removing or renaming a field or route, changing a type or meaning, tightening validation on existing data, or changing a default. It ships behind a compatibility mode for at least one release, as pagination did: omitting limit and offset still returns the bare array.
  3. Mark it. A deprecated route or field is listed in §13 with its replacement and its earliest removal release. Where practical, the response carries a Deprecation: true header.
  4. Schema. Columns are dropped only in a migration that follows the release which stopped reading them. Compatibility triggers bridge legacy writers in the meantime, as 033 and 035 do for hardware_models.
  5. Security exceptions. A change needed to close a vulnerability may skip the window. It is recorded in §13 after the fact.

The live deprecations are the panel CSRF bridge (§7.2) and the legacy hardware_models writers (§6). Both are tracked in §13.

12.6Testing matrix

Two workflow files define the same twelve jobs, including the SoR migration rehearsals: .forgejo/workflows/ci.yml (canonical) and .github/workflows/ci.yml. GitHub adds a thirteenth, macos, which builds the portable core and Unity suites as a universal binary. Run the suite for any layer you touched before pushing.

13Roadmap

This chapter lists planned interface changes, live deprecations, and the backlog that came out of writing this manual. Status chips: done shipped and live; planned scoped in an approved plan; backlog identified but not scheduled; parked deliberately deferred. Status is as of 2026-11-03.

13.1Inventory phases I1–I5

The plan of record is docs/Inventory_Remit_2026-11-01.md. Each phase is shippable on its own and cross-lab reviewed. The "API impact" column is INFERRED from the remit's design section (§4 of that document); none of I2–I5 has been designed at route level yet.

PhaseStatusDeliversAPI impact
I1doneBroadened catalogue (product_types + barcodes + backfill), location levels with uniqueness, move and merge, bulk storage units, container types, project assignment. Close-out migration 037 is live.Shipped: catalogue and location routes in §3. Migrations 033–037.
I2plannedScan dispatch, and a record view with edit and delete for items, locations and projects (R3)A dispatch endpoint that resolves one scanned string, in order: exact registry match → system-format prefix (AKO-, LOC-, PRJ-, SI-) → GTIN with check digit → manual. Soft-delete routes with audit.
I3plannedProduct lookup, and the "scan → confirm → add N items" flow (R1)A server-side PHP provider adapter (UPCitemdb free tier, then Open Products Facts) with a cache table keyed by normalised GTIN. The browser never calls the providers. Results are candidates the operator confirms.
I4plannedUnified search and editable browse views (R6)One search endpoint over product types, items, locations, codes and receipts: MariaDB FULLTEXT plus exact-code match. Likely a new FULLTEXT-index migration.
I5plannedLabel templates, verified printer profiles, share-to-vendor-app (R5)Rendered label images sized per printer (MakeID EP53, MakeID E1, Nemonic MIP-201W). There is no direct-print bridge, so this is probably a render route or client-side canvas rather than a device API.
For integrators I2–I5 are additive under §12.5. Existing catalogue, location and code routes keep their shapes. New fields may appear in responses; clients must ignore unknown fields.

AError code index

Every error uses the envelope {ok:false, error:{code, message}} (§2.6). Clients should branch on the HTTP status and the code. The message is for people and may change.

A.1Raised before a handler runs

The router, the policy layer and authentication raise these for any route. They are omitted from the per-route lists in §3.

HTTPCodeConditionSource
401unauthorizedNo valid session on a route that needs oneAuth.php
403forbiddenRole too low: viewer on an operator route, or not admin on an admin routeOperator.php via Policy
403forbidden"Cross-origin or missing Origin on a state-changing request."Policy::enforceCsrf
403forbidden"Missing or invalid CSRF token." The X-Solari-CSRF header does not match the session token.Policy::enforceCsrf
403forbidden"Unrecognized policy for …". The route declares an unknown auth level, which is a server bug; the request fails closed.Policy.php
404not_found"No route for …"Router.php
405method_not_allowedThe path exists but not for this methodRouter.php

A.2Raised by route handlers

This table is generated from the router table, like Appendix B. It lists each (status, code) pair a handler can return, how many routes return it, and up to three of those routes. The same code may appear under more than one status. For example, control_error is 400 when solariCtl rejects the arguments as invalid, 502 when the bridge replies badly, and 503 when this server is not the active primary ("active server required", "not primary", "lease lost"). A role or confirm-token refusal from the bridge becomes 403 (SolariCtl::statusForCode).

A.3Retry guidance

CodesRetry?How
503 busy, 503 service_unavailable, 502/503 control_unavailable, 503 control_errorYesBack off and retry. For queued control verbs, resend the same Idempotency-Key so a retry cannot run the action twice (§4.2).
409 conflict (stale updated_at)After re-readFetch the record again, re-apply the change, and send the new updated_at (§2.9).
409 merge_conflictAfter re-previewCall merge-preview again and send the new fingerprint.
409 queue_fullLaterThe panel command queue already holds 16 pending commands. Wait for the daemon to drain it.
409 confirm_required, 400 confirmation_requiredWith confirmationResend with the explicit confirmation field the route documents.
413 inventory_page_requiredDifferentlySwitch to the paged collection routes.
Other 400, 404, 409, 413, 415NoFix the request.
500 db_error, 500 internal_errorOnce, then reportDetails are in the PHP-FPM log, not in the response.
Since 037 Inventory writes that hit a database deadlock return 409 with a "try again" message. They are safe to retry as they are.
